**Moderation for uploaded photos reuses the existing report pipeline rather than adding anything new, per explicit instruction — there is no budget here for image classification.** `DuvodNahlaseni` gained one value, `nevhodne_foto` ("Nevhodná profilová fotka") — a plain addition to an already-unconstrained `text` column (`reports.reason` has no `CHECK`), so no migration was needed for it, unlike the Storage policies above. A moderator resolving that report today only has the existing tools (ban from Social, ban from the app) — there's no "delete just the photo" action yet, since nothing asked for one; flagged here as the natural next gap rather than built speculatively.

**Verified against real production Supabase data**: `precti_verejny_profil()` now returns `motto` for a real authenticated, non-blocked caller and still returns nothing for `anon` (the pre-existing guard, unchanged); `storage.objects` RLS lets a user insert into their own `<uid>/avatar.jpg` path, rejects the identical insert aimed at someone else's folder, and rejects `anon` outright — the same three-shape check this session applies to every new RLS surface. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a friend row and the public profile dialog both render the mocked `avatar_url` as a real `<img>`, not the initial-letter fallback; the dialog shows the mocked bio text; the report dialog's reason list includes the new option. The upload network call itself (`supabase.storage.from().upload()`) was not exercised end-to-end in the mock — `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` intercepts the REST/RPC shape, not Storage's distinct upload response contract, and `avatarStorage.ts`'s own try/catch already guarantees a failed or unmocked upload degrades to "local photo only, cloud step silently skipped" rather than a crash, consistent with how every other optional cloud path in this file is verified.

**The same pass added a second slice on top: banner, a longer bio, avatar frames, pinned badges, mutual-friend counts, friend suggestions, and a shareable profile link with QR — all still free, no paid infrastructure, because none of them needed anything beyond another `profiles` column, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function shaped like the ones already in this file, or a client-only library.**

**Bio, banner, avatar frame and pinned badges all needed the exact same "text field that mirrors to `profiles` for others to see" shape motto already has — so they ride the existing `CloudSnapshot`/`mergeSnapshots`/`cloudSync.ts` pipeline instead of a new one.** `bio`/`frameId`/`pinnedBadges` joined `ProfileData` (`useProfileData.ts`) and `CloudSnapshot` (`core/supabase/types.ts`) alongside `displayName`/`motto`, with the identical "local non-empty wins, else remote" merge rule (`merge.ts`) — a fourth field going through a proven mechanism, not a parallel one. `bannerUrl` is the one exception: like `avatar_url`, it's set by a Storage upload writing straight to `profiles.banner_url` (`core/supabase/avatarStorage.ts`'s `nahrajDoStorage`, refactored out of the avatar-only version to share the resize/upload/write steps for both), never by the debounced snapshot push — two independent write paths for the same column would race.

**`pinned_badges` taught a real lesson about trusting a client-writable column: the first version of `precti_verejny_profil()` returned it verbatim, which meant a user could claim a badge they never unlocked.** `profiles.pinned_badges` has a plain self-row `UPDATE` policy (same as every other profile field), so nothing stops a client from writing any badge id into it — the app's own picker (`RewardModule.tsx`) only ever offers unlocked badges, but that's a client-side courtesy, not a guarantee. Caught by testing the actual read path against real data (an unearned badge came back in the result) before shipping, not by inspection — the fix filters `pinned_badges` against `user_badges` inside the function itself (`unnest(...) where b in (select badge_id from user_badges where user_id = cil)`), so what the client stores is only ever a *request* to show a badge, and what ships to a viewer is only what that user actually has, verified again afterward with a real unlocked-vs-not pair.

**Avatar frames are a cosmetic recolor of the ring `SocialAvatar.tsx` already draws, not a new component.** The conic-gradient ring already exists (colored per-user via `avatarColor.ts`); a "frame" (`social/avatarFrames.ts`) is just a fixed `{a, b}` color pair overriding those two CSS custom properties, the same mechanism, different input. VIP-gating reuses `cosmetics.premium` exactly like app themes do — but with a twist themes never needed: a theme only ever applies to the *current* user (`useHasPermission` works fine), while a frame is displayed on *someone else's* avatar, where no live permission hook exists for a stranger. `resolveActiveFrameId(frameId, role)` solves this the way `precti_verejny_profil()`'s own `role` column already does the work — that role string is already time-limited correctly server-side (`valid_until is null or valid_until > now()`), so evaluating a VIP frame's eligibility from it, client-side, at render time, gets the same "expired VIP silently loses the cosmetic, no active downgrade needed" property `resolveActiveThemeId` established for themes, without inventing a second server round-trip to ask "is this stranger still VIP?"

**Mutual-friend counts and suggestions both needed a `SECURITY DEFINER` function for the same reason `precti_verejny_profil()` does — your own RLS on `friendships` only lets you read rows *you're* a party to, never a stranger's.** `pocet_spolecnych_pratel(cil)` returns only a number, deliberately — the running rule in this file (`je_muj_pritel`, `je_blokovan_se_mnou`) is never to expose a friend *list* belonging to someone else, only yes/no or count questions about it, and a mutual-friends count answers "how well do we know each other" without ever naming who. `navrhy_pratel()` is capped at 8, the same restraint `hledej_podle_jmena`'s cap of 20 already established for any function that could otherwise be used to enumerate the social graph at scale. Both are revoked from `anon`/`PUBLIC` and granted only to `authenticated`, checked directly against `anon` this time before shipping — the exact class of mistake (`ma_pravo_na_tajny_chat`, `precti_verejny_profil`'s anon guard) this file has now caught three times, caught here by habit rather than by accident.

**The friend code finally has an owner-facing screen again, and it's a materially different feature from the one that was removed, not a revival of it.** `PratelePanel.tsx`'s old "TVŮJ KÓD" UI was deliberately retired when Social moved to search-by-name (see above) — bringing back a code-entry box for *discovery* would undo that decision. What's new instead is *sharing your own profile on purpose*: a "MŮJ PROFIL" card shows your code, a copyable link (`shareLink.ts`'s `profilOdkaz`, shaped `/social?kod=...` — the same query-param pattern `AppModule.tsx` already uses for `?kategorie=`, not a new route) and a QR code rendered by the `qrcode` package (pure client-side, MIT-licensed, no network call, in keeping with this project's "runs completely without the cloud" rule same as `jszip`/`web-push` before it). `SocialModule.tsx` reads `?kod=` once on mount, resolves it through the same `najdiPodleKodu` the old UI used, opens `VerejnyProfilDialog`, and strips the parameter from the URL so reloading or navigating back doesn't reopen it.

**Scanning someone else's QR follows the exact "feature-detect, offer a typed fallback" shape `BuddyOverlay.tsx` already established for iOS Safari's missing `SpeechRecognition`.** `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx` tries `window.BarcodeDetector` (Chrome/Edge/Android; not Firefox/Safari, hence `social/barcodeTypes.d.ts` declaring just the slice of the interface used, the same reasoning as `speechTypes.d.ts`); when it's missing, or camera access fails, a manual code-entry input takes over instead of a dead end — nobody is left without a way to add a friend just because their browser or a denied permission got in the way of the faster path. Camera lifecycle (start on mount, `getTracks().forEach(t => t.stop())` on unmount) follows `usePoseEngine.ts`'s ownership discipline even though this component keeps the loop inline rather than in its own hook — simple enough (no ML model, no landmarks) not to need the extra indirection.

**Verified against real production Supabase data**: `pocet_spolecnych_pratel`/`navrhy_pratel` both return correct results for a genuine triangle of friendships (temporarily inserted, then removed) and are unreachable by `anon`; the `pinned_badges` fix confirmed against a real `user_badges` row (unlocked badge passes through, a claimed-but-unlocked one is silently dropped); the `pripnute_odznaky_max_3` `CHECK` constraint rejects a fourth id from either the client or a direct write. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): the own-profile card renders the real friend code and a QR `<img>`; a friend's dialog shows bio, the equipped frame's ring class, and one pinned-badge pill; the suggestions section shows a mocked candidate with its mutual-friend count; a `/social?kod=...` URL opens the right profile automatically. The camera-based scanner itself (`SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`'s `BarcodeDetector` path) was not exercised live — no camera in this sandbox — verified by code review and by the same feature-detection shape already proven for speech recognition instead.

**The záložky (tab) row and the "MŮJ PROFIL" card were both flagged by a real screenshot as cramped and hard to tap — five/six `flex: 1` tabs in one row forced tiny text that wrapped ("Tajný chat" broke onto two lines) with no room between tap targets.** Fixed two ways at once, both explicitly asked for. First, `.social-zalozky` switched from `flex:1`-squeezed items to a horizontally scrolling row (`overflow-x: auto`, `scroll-snap-type: x proximity`, each `.social-zalozka` back to its natural text width) — the same scrolling idiom the game hub's character carousel already established (`TvorbaPostavy.css`), not a new pattern. Second, and requested explicitly ("chci profil jako že klikneš a uvidíš profil, to má Instagram řešené") — the QR/friend-code sharing block stopped being a card wedged above "NAJÍT LIDI" in the Přátelé tab and became its own "Profil" tab, `MujProfilPanel.tsx`, the same mental model as Instagram's own profile screen: tap it, see your own profile, not someone else's list with your own card mixed in.

`MujProfilPanel.tsx` renders the same visual pieces `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` already draws for a *stranger's* profile (banner/avatar+frame/role tag/motto/bio/pinned badges/level bar/streak, sharing the same `.social-profil-*` CSS classes, wrapped in a new `.social-card--profil` modifier that gives the card the dialog's `1rem` padding so the banner's `-1rem` bleed math still lines up outside a dialog) — but unlike that dialog, it never calls `precti_verejny_profil()` over the network. For *your own* profile the data is already sitting in local stores (`useProfileData`, `useGamificationStore`, `useActiveRole`), so building it from those is both simpler and matches "offline-first" — the same reasoning `avatarFrames.ts`'s own comment on `resolveActiveFrameId` already flagged ("pro VLASTNÍ avatar appka může zavolat useHasPermission přímo," now realized via `useActiveRole` instead). It also gained one thing the dialog can't offer for a stranger: an "Upravit profil" button that navigates straight to `/profil`, since seeing your own profile without a way to edit it would be a dead end the dialog's "someone else's" version never has.

The QR/kód sharing block (`social/shareLink.ts`'s `profilOdkaz`, the `qrcode` package, `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`) moved out of `PratelePanel.tsx` into `MujProfilPanel.tsx` unchanged in content — same code, new home. `PratelePanel.tsx` keeps only what's actually about *other* people: search, suggestions, requests, the friends list. A new `user` icon (single figure, distinct from `users`, the group icon "Přátelé" already uses) was added to `SocialIcon.tsx` for the tab.

**A real screenshot of the new Profil tab caught a second, unrelated bug: a locally-uploaded avatar photo bled out below the circular ring instead of cropping to it, tens of pixels of the photo spilling into the motto/level rows underneath.** `.social-avatar`'s box comes from `inset: 3px` alone, no explicit `width`/`height` — that's fine for the plain `<span>` initial (a non-replaced element, which stretches to fill an inset box with all four sides set), but the photo path renders a real `<img>`, a *replaced* element, and replaced elements size themselves from their own intrinsic aspect ratio when width/height aren't explicit, ignoring the geometric gap between the insets entirely — a classic, easy-to-miss CSS distinction between replaced and non-replaced elements. A tall, narrow source photo (the common case for a phone screenshot chosen as an avatar) rendered at its own intrinsic ratio instead of clipping to the 50×50 circle. Fixed with one line: `.social-avatar--foto` now also sets `width: 100%; height: 100%` explicitly, which forces the replaced element to size itself from the box (not its intrinsic ratio), letting the pre-existing `object-fit: cover` actually do its job. Confirmed with a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) seeding a deliberately tall SVG data URI straight into `secureStorage`'s encrypted format and reading the rendered `<img>`'s real bounding box: 56×196 before the fix, 56×56 (matching the wrap exactly) after.

**Přátelé stopped being its own tab entirely and folded into Profil, on top of a straight reorder — both explicitly asked for in the same pass.** `PratelePanel.tsx` (search/suggestions/requests/friends list) is now rendered *by* `MujProfilPanel.tsx`, appended right after the "MŮJ KÓD" sharing block, rather than being a sibling `SocialModule.tsx` switches between — one `<PratelePanel>` render call, nested `.social-panel` flex column inside another, no new wrapper needed since the nesting is visually harmless (same gap, same card styling). `MujProfilPanel` gained an `onOtevritChat` prop to pass through to it (the "Napsat" action on a friend row still needs to open the real chat). The friend-request badge count (`cekaZadosti`) moved with it — it now lights up the `profil` tab id in `SocialModule.tsx`'s odznak switch, not a `pratele` id that no longer exists. `ZALOZKY` dropped the `pratele` entry and `profil` moved from last to first — with search-and-friends now living inside it, Profil is the de facto home screen of Social, not a sharing card tucked in at the end, so the default active tab (`useState<Zalozka>`) changed from `'pratele'` to `'profil'` too.

**Social is being redesigned in explicit phases (requested as such), the first being a full navigation rework: a bottom tab bar (Instagram/TikTok-style) in place of the old horizontal row of tabs above the content.** This isn't cosmetic-only — it's also what finally, structurally, retires the wrapping/crowding problem the horizontal-scroll fix (above) only patched: five or six `flex: 1` labelled pills fighting for one row's width was the root cause, and a bottom bar's icon-first, one-word-label items never had that problem to begin with, so `.social-bottom-nav`'s items go back to plain `flex: 1` with no scroll container needed. `.social-nav-item` stacks `SocialIcon` above a small label (`.social-nav-icon-wrap` wrapping just the icon, so the unread-badge — `.social-nav-odznak` — anchors to the icon's own corner, not the whole button); the active tab tints cyan, matching the app's existing selection-state colour everywhere else. The bar itself reuses the Hub's own already-safe-area-tested `.hub-bottom-bar` shape verbatim rather than inventing a second pattern for the same problem: `margin-top: auto` inside `.social-page`'s flex column pushes it to the bottom with no scrolling needed, `position: sticky; bottom: 0` keeps it there once content *does* scroll, and a `::before` pseudo-element bleeds a gradient backdrop past the page's own padding (negative `inset` matching `.social-page`'s exact padding numbers) so content sliding underneath fades rather than hard-cuts. `.social-toast`'s fixed `bottom` offset moved from `1.25rem` to `4.5rem` for the same reason — it used to float safely in empty space at the bottom of the page; now that space is occupied by real navigation, and the old offset would have sat the toast on top of the tab bar. The top bar lost its subtitle and breathing hero emoji in the same pass — both belonged to the old top-heavy layout, and a bottom-nav'd screen only needs a bare wordmark up top, the same minimal header shape Instagram/TikTok themselves use. Verified with a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing): four nav items render (no horizontal scroll needed even conceptually now), tapping between them switches the active tab and its highlighted state correctly, and a full-page screenshot confirmed the bar sits flush at the bottom with the gradient backdrop reading correctly against scrolled content. Further Social redesign phases — Stories (24h posts), photo/video messages in chat, voice messages — are planned as separate, later passes, not bundled into this one.

**The four bottom-nav items landed on Profil/Chaty/Vyhledávač/Nastavení in a follow-up pass, explicitly requested with an Instagram/TikTok comparison for the search tab** — a real, dedicated discovery screen, not a card wedged above the friends list. This meant re-splitting Profil's content a second time in as many passes: search-by-name, suggestions, and both directions of pending friend requests moved out of `PratelePanel.tsx` into a new `VyhledavacPanel.tsx` (its own bottom-nav tab, `search` icon — a new `SocialIcon` case, magnifying-glass shape matching the existing feather-icon set), leaving `PratelePanel.tsx` with only the already-established friends list (still rendered *inside* `MujProfilPanel.tsx`, unchanged from the previous pass). The unread-request badge (`cekaZadosti`) moved with the requests it counts, from the `profil` tab id to `vyhledavac`.

**Blokovaní and Hlášení lost their bottom-nav slots entirely and became a menu inside a new "Nastavení" tab — Social's own settings, not the app's `/nastaveni`.** `NastaveniPanel.tsx` is the same "menu of rows → full-screen section with its own back button" shape `AdminModule.tsx` already established for the admin panel (`.social-nastaveni-radek`, an `arrow-left` icon flipped 180° via a new `.social-nastaveni-sipka` class rather than adding a dedicated chevron icon — one fewer icon to maintain for a shape `SocialIcon.tsx` already has). This is also what let the old fifth, *conditional* bottom-nav slot for Tajný chat go away: it's now one more row in this same menu, shown only when `tajnyStav.smim` is true, exactly like every other permission-gated row in this codebase (the admin panel's own tabs, the game hub's roster). The practical win is that the bottom nav is now a stable four items for literally everyone — a VIP/moderator/admin no longer gets a bar with a different item count than everyone else's, which the old five-or-four-item version always did. `NastaveniPanel` still needs `onOtevritTajnyChat` threaded from `SocialModule.tsx` (the same `setOtevrenyTajnyChat` the old top-level `'tajne'` tab used) since opening an actual secret chat still has to happen at `SocialModule`'s level, above the tab switch, exactly as before. The pending-secret-chat-invite badge (`tajnyStav.cekajiciNaMe`) now shows two places: on the `nastaveni` bottom-nav icon itself (so it's discoverable without opening the menu) and again on the "Tajný chat" row inside the menu (so it's still clear *which* row has something waiting) — deliberately not deduplicated, since they answer different questions ("does Nastavení have something for me" vs. "which row").

Verified the same way as the previous pass: temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) confirming all four new labels render, tapping into Vyhledávač and Nastavení switches content correctly, drilling into "Blokovaní" from the Nastavení menu shows the real panel, and "← Zpět do nastavení" returns to the menu — plus full-page screenshots checked by eye for each screen. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (this pass touched no routing).

**A real screenshot of the redesigned Profil tab caught the default avatar's placeholder icon (head + shoulders) looking visibly off-centre inside its circular ring** — `useProfileData.ts`'s `DEFAULT_AVATAR_SVG`, drawn once and reused everywhere `profile.avatar` falls back to it. The shoulders shape touched the very bottom edge of its 96×96 viewBox with zero margin, while the head sat 23px clear of the top — asymmetric padding that read as "posed too low" once actually cropped into a circle. Fixed by shifting the whole icon (head circle + shoulders path) up 11 units so both margins land close to equal (~11–12px each); horizontal centring was already correct and untouched. Confirmed with a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) screenshotting the rendered avatar directly.

**The same follow-up caught a second avatar issue, this one about the colour *ring* rather than the icon inside it: part of the ring could disappear against its own content.** `avatarColor.ts` hashes a user's id into two hues (offset by the golden angle) completely independent of whatever the avatar *content* actually looks like — for the default avatar specifically, that content is a fixed blue→violet gradient (`DEFAULT_AVATAR_SVG`), so any user whose hash happened to land near blue or violet got a ring that visually merged into the avatar behind it for that whole arc, reading as "the ring is hidden behind the avatar" rather than as a coincidence of colour. The actual gap between the ring's masked band (`.social-avatar-prstenec`, the outer 2.5px) and the avatar content (`.social-avatar`, inset `3px`) was only ~0.5px — nowhere near enough to register as a visible seam regardless of colour, so any two adjacent hues blended smoothly right through it. Fixed by giving `.social-avatar-wrap` an opaque `background: var(--bg-base)` and pushing `.social-avatar`'s inset from `3px` to `5px`, opening a real ~2.5px moat of solid background between ring and content — the same "status ring needs a backing colour, not just a mask" idiom apps with coloured avatar rings commonly use. This fixes it for every avatar everywhere `SocialAvatar.tsx` renders (photos included, not just the default icon), not only the specific case that surfaced it. Confirmed the same way: a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) screenshotting the avatar directly showed the ring fully visible 360° around, no arc swallowed by the content behind it.

**Social's remaining redesign work is being finished feature-by-feature, starting with photo/video messages in chat — the first of three explicitly-requested pieces (the other two, voice messages and Stories, are separate later passes).** Unlike `avatary` (public, per the reasoning under Fáze 1 above), the new `chat-media` Storage bucket is **private**: chat content is only ever meant for the chat's own members, so `messages` gained `media_path`/`media_type` columns holding just a Storage *path*, never a full URL — `api.ts`'s `ziskejUrlMedia()` exchanges that path for a short-lived signed URL only at render time, cached in memory for under an hour so scrolling through history doesn't re-request one per visible bubble. RLS on `storage.objects` for this bucket mirrors `messages`' own INSERT policy almost exactly (`jsem_clenem` on the path's first segment, which is always the chat id, plus `je_skutecny_ucet()`/`not je_zabanovan_ze_social()`), and SELECT is scoped the same way — a signed-URL request for someone else's chat simply can't succeed, verified against real production data via role/JWT impersonation (a genuine member inserting and reading successfully, a non-member rejected on both INSERT and SELECT, `anon` rejected outright).

**`messages.body` still can't be empty (`CHECK length(body) >= 1`), so a media message with no caption gets a synthetic body instead of a relaxed constraint** — `VYCHOZI_POPISEK_MEDIA` (`types.ts`, `'📷 Fotka'` / `'🎥 Video'`) is shared between `api.ts` (what actually gets written) and `ChatView.tsx` (which suppresses re-displaying it as if it were a real caption someone typed, showing the media itself instead). This was deliberate over widening the CHECK constraint: a non-null, human-readable `body` is what every other reader of this table (search, notifications, moderation tooling) already assumes, and a media-aware exception there would be a second thing every future reader has to remember.

**Client-side, an image gets resized before upload the same way an avatar/banner already does (`fileToResizedBlob`, `core/supabase/avatarStorage.ts`'s established helper) — video does not, since there's no cheap way to transcode video in the browser; only a client-side size check (25 MB, matching the bucket's own `file_size_limit`) guards it before the upload even starts.** `nahratChatMedium`/`ziskejUrlMedia` live in `social/api.ts` itself, not a sibling module — unlike `avatarStorage.ts`, which earns its separate location by being profile infrastructure `ProfilModule.tsx` also calls directly, this is Social-specific end to end, so it follows "`api.ts` is the only place Social talks to Supabase" rather than repeating `avatarStorage.ts`'s exception for a case that doesn't share its justification.

**A real screenshot caught a second instance of a CSS mistake this session had already made once, on avatars — a replaced element (`<img>`/`<video>`) sized only by `max-width`/`max-height` renders at its own intrinsic size when that's smaller than the cap, not at the cap.** `.social-bublina-media` had exactly that shape; a small source image (or, tellingly, the tiny 1×1 test PNG used to verify this in Playwright) rendered at a few physical pixels instead of filling a reasonable preview box. Fixed by giving it an explicit `width`/`height` (240×200, `max-width: 100%` for narrow screens) with `object-fit: cover` doing the cropping — the same lesson as the avatar fix, generalized: a replaced element needs an explicit size to fill a box, `max-*` alone only ever shrinks, never grows.

**Verifying this against the mock took an honest look at what `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` could and couldn't do, and extended it rather than working around it, because the next two phases (voice messages, then Stories) need the exact same capability.** The generic `/rest/v1` mock had no concept of Storage's sign-URL endpoint, which has a completely different response shape (`{signedURL: "..."}`, a path fragment the client itself turns into an absolute URL) — routing it through the generic fallback silently produced a syntactically-valid but garbage URL (the literal string `"undefined"` spliced in), which `ziskejUrlMedia` didn't catch either, since `data` itself wasn't falsy. Both gaps got fixed together: `ziskejUrlMedia` now checks `data?.signedUrl` truthiness explicitly, not just `!data`, and `mockSupabase()` gained a real `/storage/v1/object/sign/` handler plus a matching `/mock-signed/` GET route that actually serves image bytes (a 1×1 PNG) — so a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) could confirm the whole round trip: a mocked message row with `media_path` set renders a correctly-sized bubble, not a blank one silently failing.

**Fáze 2/3 of Social's remaining redesign work — voice messages — reuses every piece of the photo/video infrastructure above rather than building a parallel system: same `chat-media` bucket, same `media_path`/`media_type` columns (just a third value, `'audio'`, added to the CHECK and to the bucket's `allowed_mime_types`), same `ziskejUrlMedia()` signed-URL path.** The only genuinely new piece is *producing* the file in the first place — recording, not picking one that already exists — so that's where the actual new code is: `MediaRecorder`, feature-detected once at module load (`PODPORUJE_NAHRAVANI_HLASU`, the same "detect once, hide the affordance entirely if unsupported" shape as `BuddyOverlay.tsx`'s speech recognition and `SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`'s barcode scanner — there's no sensible typed fallback for "record your voice," so hiding the mic button is the honest degradation, not a compromise). `nahratChatMedium()` in `api.ts` grew a third branch (`file.type.startsWith('audio/')`) sharing the same 25 MB cap video already had — deliberately one shared limit rather than a separate, lower one for audio, since one number is simpler to reason about than two and voice messages are short enough in practice never to approach it.

**The record button replaces the send button, not the attach button, and only while the composer is empty — the same convention Messenger/WhatsApp already established, not a new idiom this app invented.** Typing anything makes the mic disappear and the ordinary send button reappear in its place, so there's never a moment where "what does this button do" is ambiguous between finishing a text message and starting a voice one. Recording itself replaces the *entire* composer with a distinct row (pulsing dot, live `mm:ss`, cancel, send) rather than layering a recording state on top of the normal form — attaching a photo or typing while a voice note is mid-recording was never a real use case worth the complexity of supporting concurrently. `MediaRecorder.isTypeSupported()` picks the best available container (`audio/webm;codecs=opus` first, falling back through `audio/webm` and `audio/mp4`) since Chrome and Safari support non-overlapping sets — the extension written to Storage (`webm` vs `m4a`) follows whichever the browser actually recorded, not a hardcoded assumption.

**Playback is a small custom player (play/pause circle, progress bar, elapsed time), not the browser's native `<audio controls>`** — deliberately, because the built-in control strip is wide and desktop-styled in every browser that ships one, and doesn't fit a ~180px chat bubble without looking visibly foreign next to the rest of the app's own design language (unlike `<video controls>` for the photo/video work above, which is compact enough on mobile to keep as-is). The real `<audio>` element stays in the DOM purely as a playback source; its own UI is never shown. Leaving the chat mid-recording — or mid-anything — releases the microphone the same way `usePoseEngine.ts` already guarantees for the camera: a dedicated cleanup `useEffect` stops every track and any still-running `MediaRecorder`, so navigating away never leaves the mic indicator lit in the background.

**Verified the same way as the photo/video pass**, reusing the same extended mock: a message row with `media_type: 'audio'` renders the custom player (not a broken/blank bubble), and the mic-vs-send button swap was checked directly — mic visible with an empty composer, gone the instant text is typed, send button visible in its place — via a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing). Actually recording through a real microphone wasn't exercised (no mic in this sandbox, same limitation the QR scanner's camera path already accepted) — verified by code review and by the same feature-detection shape already proven elsewhere in this file.

**Fáze 3/3 — Stories — is the last of the three Social items done in this pass, and unlike the photo/video/voice work above it is genuinely new infrastructure, not a reuse of `messages`/`chat-media`: a story belongs to no chat, it's one post visible to every friend at once, so it gets its own tables (`stories`, `story_views`) and its own private bucket (`stories`), not a third `media_type` bolted onto an existing one.** Expiry follows the exact "belt and suspenders" shape `tajne_zpravy` already established: the `stories` SELECT policy hides any row past `expiruje_at` immediately (`expiruje_at > now() and (user_id = auth.uid() or (je_muj_pritel(user_id) and not je_blokovan_se_mnou(user_id)))` — the author's *own* expired story is hidden too, on purpose, the same "the policy is the real deadline, not a client-side filter" posture as the secret chat's messages), and `smaz_expirovane_stories()` (`SECURITY DEFINER`) does the actual deletion later, called client-side and best-effort (`.then(()=>{}, ()=>{})`, errors swallowed) from `nactiStories()` on every load — nothing breaks if the call is skipped or fails, the policy alone already makes an expired story unreachable.

**`smaz_expirovane_stories()` repeated, and this time caught before shipping by checking the grants directly rather than trusting `get_advisors`, the exact class of mistake this file has now documented three times (`ma_pravo_na_tajny_chat`, `precti_verejny_profil`'s anon guard).** Postgres — or more precisely, this project's own `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES` setup for the `public` schema — grants `EXECUTE` to `anon`/`authenticated` on every newly created function by default, not just to `PUBLIC`; the migration's `revoke ... from public` left `anon`'s own separate grant standing (`proacl` still showed `anon=X/postgres` after the "fix"), so the cleanup function really was callable by an unauthenticated caller until a second, explicit `revoke execute ... from anon` closed it. `get_advisors` (security) reported zero lints throughout — a function that only deletes rows it's already allowed to delete doesn't trip the linter's checks, so this one was only caught by directly querying `has_function_privilege('anon', ...)` before calling it done, the same discipline `ma_pravo_na_tajny_chat` was eventually caught with.

**A story's viewer list is deliberately visible only to its own author, and only as names, not as a count anyone else can query.** `story_views` SELECT policy is `viewer_id = auth.uid() or exists (select 1 from stories s where s.id = story_id and s.user_id = auth.uid())` — a stranger recording their own view can always read that one row back (needed so the client can show "already seen" state), but nobody except the author can enumerate who else looked. `oznacitZhlednuti()` inserts and lets a `23505` (viewer already recorded, `primary key (story_id, viewer_id)`) pass silently — the exact same "double-tap is a no-op success, not an error" shape `pridatReakci()` already established for message reactions, reused here rather than re-invented.

**`ziskejUrlMedia()` became `ziskejPodepsanouUrl(bucket, path)` with two thin exports (`ziskejUrlMedia` for `chat-media`, `ziskejUrlStory` for `stories`), not two independent copies of the same caching function.** The in-memory signed-URL cache is keyed on `` `${bucket}/${path}` ``, not just `path`, now that a second private bucket exists — cheap insurance against two different buckets' paths ever colliding in the shared cache, even though in practice a chat's uuid and a user's uuid living in the same pool make an actual collision implausible.

**Building this against the mock surfaced a real bug in the shared `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` sign-URL handling that the photo/video pass's own test never exercised strongly enough to catch.** `createSignedUrl()`'s real implementation composes the final URL as `` `${this.url}${signedURL}` ``, where the storage client's own `this.url` *already* ends in `/storage/v1` — so the mock's sign handler, which returned `signedURL: '/mock-signed/<path>'`, actually produced a browser request to `/storage/v1/mock-signed/<path>`, not `/mock-signed/<path>` as the second matching branch assumed. The mismatch meant every signed-image request silently fell through to the generic REST fallback, which answered with `[]`/`application/json` — a JSON array where the `<img>` expected image bytes, rendering as a genuinely `0×0`, invisible element. The photo/video pass's own verification never caught this because it checked that the `<img>` existed in the DOM, not that it actually had a non-zero rendered size; this pass's stricter `toBeVisible()` + `boundingBox()` assertion did. Fixed by matching the real composed path (`/storage/v1/mock-signed/` instead of bare `/mock-signed/`) in the shared, permanent helper — not worked around in the throwaway spec — since Stories was exactly the kind of "next feature needs the same capability" case the photo/video pass's own comment on `helpers.ts` had anticipated.

**`StoryProhlizec.tsx` (the full-screen viewer) and `PridatStoryDialog.tsx` (the creation flow) are this app's first use of `createPortal`, and the reason is a real, previously-latent bug this pass's own stricter visibility test caught, not a stylistic preference.** `.social-panel` carries `position: relative` (for its own z-index above the ambient background scene), which makes it a genuine CSS stacking context; a `position: fixed` full-screen overlay rendered as its descendant still visually spans the whole viewport (fixed positioning itself isn't affected, since nothing in the ancestor chain has `transform`/`filter` to hijack fixed's *containing block* the way `backdrop-filter` would), but its `z-index` only ever gets compared against its *siblings inside that same stacking context* — never against `.social-bottom-nav`, a sibling of `.social-panel` itself with an equal `z-index: 1` and a later DOM position, which therefore painted on top of the story viewer regardless of the viewer's own `z-index: 60`. Confirmed visually before the fix (a screenshot showing the bottom nav bar rendered over the black story overlay) and fixed by having both components `createPortal(..., document.body)` instead of rendering in place — the correct general fix for "this needs to be above literally everything," not a one-off CSS patch, and the first place in the codebase this pattern was needed since every earlier full-screen dialog (`SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`, `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`, etc.) happens to be small/centered enough that the same latent stacking bug never became visible enough to notice.

**The stories bar sits at the very top of the Profil tab, above the profile card itself — the same "stories row above your own feed" position Instagram uses, and the account's own tile always comes first.** `StoriesBar.tsx` reads `useStories()` (a small dedicated hook, `useStories.ts`, following the exact shape `useTajnyChat.ts` already established for "its own hook, not folded into `useSocial.ts`" — except here the reason isn't a permission gate, since every signed-in account can post a story, it's simply that nothing outside this one screen needs the data). The account's own tile is a `<span>` wrapping two **sibling** `<button>`s — one covering the avatar (opens the viewer if a story exists, otherwise does nothing, `disabled`) and a small "+" badge overlaid on its corner that always opens the file picker — the same "sibling, not nested button" shape `PostavaKarta`'s delete button already established in the game hub, chosen for the identical reason: a `<button>` inside a `<button>` is invalid HTML and breaks keyboard navigation.

**A friend's ring is a genuine second ring drawn *around* `SocialAvatar`'s own existing one, not a restyling of it** — `SocialAvatar.tsx` already draws a per-user gradient ring for identity ("whose avatar is this"), and Stories needed a completely different signal on the same avatar ("does this person have something unseen"), so `.social-story-krouzek` wraps it in one more masked ring, colour-coded (a fixed magenta→cyan→violet conic gradient) when at least one story is unseen, flat `var(--border-strong)` gray once every story in that person's group has been viewed (`StorySkupina.vsechnyZhlednute`, computed once in `nactiStories()` from a single `story_views` query filtered to the viewer's own rows, not recomputed per-render). The gap between the two rings (`.social-story-krouzek-mezera`, `background: var(--bg-base)`) is the same "a status ring needs a backing colour, not just a mask" fix this session already had to make once on the avatar's own inner ring — applied here from the start instead of being a second bug to catch later.

**Creating a story is one dialog, not two steps across two screens** — picking a file immediately opens `PridatStoryDialog.tsx` full-screen with a live preview (`URL.createObjectURL`, revoked on unmount) and an optional caption input, and `api.pridatStory()` resizes (`fileToResizedBlob`, the same helper avatar/banner/chat-photo uploads already share), uploads, and inserts the row in one call — no separate "confirm" step the way `zaloz_chat()`'s orphan-row concern required elsewhere in this file, because a story has no second table anything else could be left pointing at if the insert failed after a successful upload; an orphaned Storage object with no `stories` row is simply unreachable through the app, the same accepted cost as an orphaned `chat-media` blob left behind by a soft-deleted message.

**Deleting a story is hard, not soft like a chat message** — `smazatStory()` is a plain `.delete().eq('id', id)`, RLS-gated to `user_id = auth.uid()`. A story has no replies pointing at it the way a chat message might, so there is no conversational context a soft-delete would need to preserve; keeping a phantom row around just to say "there used to be a story here" would serve nobody. The viewer (`StoryProhlizec.tsx`) shows a trash-can button only to the story's own author, confirms with `window.confirm` (the same "irreversible, one misclick away" guard `SpravaSkupinyDialog.tsx`'s member removal and the chat leave button already use elsewhere in Social), and advances to the next story in the group (or closes, if that was the last one) rather than leaving the viewer open on a story that no longer exists.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation, the same three-shape check this file applies to every new RLS surface**: a genuine friend reads a non-expired story and is rejected once it (or their own) has expired; a non-friend and a blocked friend are both rejected; `anon` is rejected outright with a permission error (not silently empty) on `stories`, `story_views`, and the `stories` bucket's `storage.objects` policies alike; `story_views` INSERT only succeeds for a story the inserting viewer can actually see, and a stranger cannot read another viewer's row. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): both the account's own "add a story" tile and a friend's colour-ringed tile render; opening the friend's story shows its caption and a correctly-sized image (the assertion that caught the `helpers.ts` sign-URL bug above); adding a story through the real upload→insert flow unlocks the account's own tile once `nactiStories()` actually refetches (not merely once the creation dialog closes — closing and the underlying refetch are two different async steps, and the test had to wait on the tile's own `disabled` state rather than the dialog's absence to avoid a race); and deleting a just-added story closes the viewer. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected (this pass touched no routing).

**The bottom nav's "Profil" button stopped opening a Social-internal screen and now leaves Social entirely, landing on the app's one real profile (`pages/profil/ProfilModule.tsx`) — because having two different "this is you" screens (Social's own copy of name/avatar/level, and the app's own) was confusing, not because either one was wrong on its own.** `MujProfilPanel.tsx` — Social's former Profil tab, holding a duplicate profile-summary card, the "MŮJ KÓD" QR/friend-code share, and `PratelePanel.tsx`'s accepted-friends list — is deleted outright, not left half-wired: the button that used to call `setZalozka('profil')` now calls `navigate('/profil')` directly, staying in its original leftmost slot in the bottom nav so the row doesn't visually reflow, just changes what tapping it does. `Zalozka` (the type driving Social's own tab-switch state) dropped `'profil'` entirely — it was never a real "tab" once nothing in Social's own state machine needed to render it.

**The QR/friend-code sharing card and the accepted-friends list moved into `ProfilModule.tsx` itself, as `pages/profil/components/PratelSekce.tsx` — reusing `PratelePanel.tsx`/`SkenovatKodDialog.tsx`/`VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` verbatim rather than rebuilding equivalent UI a second time.** These three components already only depend on a `SocialStav` (from `useSocial()`) and a couple of callbacks, not on anything specific to `SocialModule.tsx` itself, so nothing about them needed to change to be called from a different page — `PratelSekce.tsx` calls `useSocial()` on its own, exactly the same hook Social itself uses, just a second independent instance of it (a second `nactiPratele()`/`nactiZadosti()`/etc. round trip when the user visits both pages in one session, an accepted cost of the two screens no longer being the same component). The two places that used to receive a resolved chat id to jump straight into a conversation (`PratelePanel`'s "Napsat" button, `VerejnyProfilDialog`'s own) both simplify to `navigate('/social')` here — the chat already exists server-side by the time either callback fires (`api.otevritChatSPritelem` creates/finds it first), so the user just has to tap it in the Chaty tab rather than landing inside it directly; not worth building a "deep-link straight into chat X" mechanism (which doesn't exist anywhere in the app yet) for this one entry point.

**`PratelSekce.tsx` is loaded via `React.lazy`, the first time anything inside `pages/profil/` has needed it — because `ProfilModule.tsx`, unlike `SocialModule.tsx`/`GameModule.tsx`, was never behind `React.lazy` to begin with, and pulling in `useSocial`, `social/api.ts` and three Social components as a plain top-level import would have put all of that Supabase/friend/chat machinery in the app's eagerly-loaded main bundle for every single visit to the app's own profile page — including for a user who never opens Social at all.** Confirmed by the actual build output, not assumed: the main `index-*.js` chunk grew by well under 1 KB after this change, and Rollup split the Social-API weight into its own `PratelSekce-*.js` chunk (~30 KB gzipped) plus a smaller shared chunk reused by `SocialModule`'s own lazy entry — the same "pay only if you actually open it" discipline `Explorace3D.tsx` already established in the game hub, applied here to a second, unrelated page.

**The vacated middle slot in Social's bottom nav went to a new "Domů" tab (`DomuPanel.tsx`), not left empty** — five items now (Profil, Chaty, **Domů**, Vyhledávač, Nastavení), Domů genuinely centered rather than tacked onto either end. It's the new default tab (`useState<Zalozka>('domu')`), replacing what `'profil'` used to be as Social's own landing screen. Its content today is deliberately thin: just `StoriesBar.tsx` (moved here unchanged from the deleted `MujProfilPanel.tsx` — same props, same behavior, nothing about Stories itself changed) plus one empty-state card explicitly saying more is coming later — not a placeholder rushed full of something that doesn't belong here yet, matching this file's own standing practice of shipping a stub that says what it is (the Arena-only combat locations' "zatím nikam nevede" sheet copy, the admin panel's `BRZY` toggles) rather than either an empty screen with no explanation or content invented just to fill the space.

**Verified with a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing) and a full-page screenshot checked by eye: Social opens straight to Domů with the story bar visible and no internal Profil screen reachable at all; the bottom nav shows all five labels in the right order; tapping Profil actually navigates to `/profil` (`toHaveURL`); the lazy `PratelSekce` section renders both "MŮJ KÓD" and the accepted-friends list on the real profile page, with the mocked friend's row visible. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts`'s four cases unaffected.

**The app's own profile page is being rebuilt toward an Instagram-style layout, requested from a rough hand-drawn mockup (centered avatar, `@username`, bio, a Followers/Following/Posts stat row, a Follow button, and a Posts/Videos/Saved tabbed grid) — started with the account's own profile, the same "own profile first, stranger's profile later" phasing this session already used for Stories.** Two genuinely new backend systems had to exist before any of that UI meant anything: one-way **follows** (a follower relationship that needs no request or acceptance, unlike `friendships`) and permanent **posts** (unlike Stories, which still exist unmodified and unrelated — a post never expires, and belongs to the profile grid, not a 24 h ring). `PratelSekce.tsx` — the lazy component this session had just created to hold the QR/friend-code card and friends list — was renamed to `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` and extended to hold this new stat row/tabs/grid too, rather than adding a second, separate lazy chunk: both need the same `useSocial()` instance, and splitting them would have meant either two redundant network round trips or awkward prop-drilling between sibling lazy components for no real benefit.

**`follows` is deliberately its own table, not a repurposing of `friendships`** — the two rows mean genuinely different things (a fact one side asserted unilaterally vs. a relationship both sides agreed to), and folding a one-way edge into a table whose `status` column already means `pending`/`accepted`/`declined` would have muddied both. RLS follows the exact restraint this file has applied to every relationship table so far: a plain self-row `SELECT` policy (`follower_id = auth.uid() or following_id = auth.uid()`) lets the client learn only "do I follow them" / "do they follow me" for one account at a time — never a stranger's full follower or following *list*, matching the same posture `pocet_spolecnych_pratel()` already established for mutual friends (count is public, the list behind it never is). Two new `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, `pocet_sledujicich()`/`pocet_sledovanych()`, answer the public "how many" question any profile's stat row needs, for anyone's account, not just the caller's own — plain aggregate counts carry none of the enumeration risk a row-by-row list would. `INSERT` requires `follower_id = auth.uid()`, `je_skutecny_ucet()`, `not je_zabanovan_ze_social()`, and (new here) `not je_blokovan_se_mnou(following_id)` — a blocked pair can't follow each other in either direction, the same posture blocking already has everywhere else in Social. A `follows_no_self` `CHECK` constraint backs up what the `INSERT` policy would already imply, belt-and-suspenders in the same spirit as this file's other invariants. `DELETE` (unfollowing) is self-row only — only the follower can remove the edge; the person being followed has no "remove this follower" action, because nothing asked for one yet.

**The exact mistake this file has now caught four times — a `SECURITY DEFINER` function keeping its default `PUBLIC`/`anon` `EXECUTE` grant after an incomplete `revoke` — was caught a fourth time on `pocet_sledujicich()`/`pocet_sledovanych()`, and this time by checking `has_function_privilege` immediately after every migration rather than waiting for `get_advisors` to flag it (which, as with `smaz_expirovane_stories()` before it, it never does for a function that only returns an aggregate — there's nothing in a `count(*)` that the linter reads as a leak).** Both functions now explicitly `revoke ... from public, anon` and `grant ... to authenticated` in the same migration, verified against real production data (`anon_exec: false`) before anything else in this feature was built on top of them.

**`posts` reuses the public-bucket posture `avatary` already established (public `SELECT`, own-folder-only write), not the private posture `chat-media`/`stories` use** — a post is meant to be visible to anyone who opens the profile, the same sensitivity level a profile photo already has, not a conversation or a friends-only story. The table itself carries no plain `SELECT` policy at all, though — reading goes through `nacti_prispevky(cil)`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, for the same reason `precti_verejny_profil()` does even though `profiles` itself is public-*ish*: one narrow, auditable read path is easier to reason about and harden later (say, if private accounts ever become a thing) than a blanket table-wide policy that every future query has to independently trust. Given the function excludes blocked accounts via `je_blokovan_se_mnou(cil)`, it repeats — deliberately guarded against, not repeated — the exact anonymous-caller gap `ma_pravo_na_tajny_chat` and `precti_verejny_profil` both shipped with the first time: `je_blokovan_se_mnou(cil)` alone evaluates `auth.uid()` as `NULL` for an unauthenticated caller and finds no matching block, so `not je_blokovan_se_mnou(cil)` alone would read `true` for `anon`. `nacti_prispevky()` adds the explicit `auth.uid() is not null` guard from the start this time, verified directly (`anon` gets a permission-denied error, not an empty result) before it shipped, rather than being caught after the fact a fourth time.

**Posting is one call, same "no orphan-row hazard" shape `pridatStory()` already established** — `nahratPrispevek()` resizes an image (`fileToResizedBlob`, the same helper avatar/banner/story/chat-photo uploads all already share) or passes video through as-is with only a client-side 25 MB size check (matching `chat-media`'s own video cap), uploads to the account's own folder in `posts`, then inserts the row; nothing else in the schema points at a post, so a failed insert after a successful upload just leaves an orphaned, unreachable Storage object, the same accepted cost documented for Stories and chat media. Deleting is hard, not soft (`smazatPrispevek()`, plain `.delete().eq('id', id)`, RLS-gated to the post's own author) — same reasoning as Stories: nothing else in the schema references a post's id, so there's no conversational context a soft-delete would need to preserve.

**The profile page's stat row, tabs and grid live entirely inside `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`, not spread across the page** — `useEffect` loads both `nactiPrispevky(mujId)` and `nactiVztahSledovani(mujId)` once the account id is known; the grid filters the already-loaded `prispevky` array by `mediaType` per tab client-side rather than re-querying per tab, since the whole list is small enough to hold in memory and this avoids repeat round trips for what's really just a display filter. "Uloženo" (Saved) is a real third tab in the UI with no backing feature at all — shown as an explicit "coming soon" empty state, the same "say what it is, don't invent filler or hide the future" posture `DomuPanel.tsx`'s own empty card and the admin panel's `BRZY` toggles already established, rather than silently omitting the tab the mockup asked for. A grid tile opens `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`, a small full-screen viewer (image or `<video controls>`, caption, delete) — since this phase only ever renders *your own* grid, the delete button has no "is this my post?" guard the way `StoryProhlizec.tsx`'s does for stories, which can belong to a friend.

**`PridatPrispevekDialog.tsx` and `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` both render via `createPortal(..., document.body)` from the start, not discovered as a bug after shipping** — the exact stacking-context problem `StoryProhlizec.tsx`/`PridatStoryDialog.tsx` hit and fixed earlier this session (a `position: relative` ancestor, here `.social-panel`, becomes its own stacking context, so a `position: fixed` overlay's `z-index` only ever gets compared against siblings *inside* that context, never against a page-level element like a bottom nav) applies identically here, and there was no reason to relearn it the hard way twice in one session.

**The profile page's own header — banner, avatar, name, bio — switched from a left-aligned row (avatar left, text stacked to its right) to a centered column, and the avatar grew from 46px to 78px, matching the mockup's "hero" identity block instead of the previous compact list-row treatment.** Purely a CSS change (`.profil-user-main`'s `flex-direction: row` → `column`, `align-items`/`text-align: center`) — none of the underlying data or edit affordances (avatar/banner upload pencils, the existing settings-menu rows) moved or changed behavior, just how the same elements stack.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a genuine follow succeeds and a repeat attempt no-ops on the unique-key collision (`23505`, same "already true, not an error" shape `pridatReakci()`/`oznacitZhlednuti()` already established); self-follow is rejected by the `CHECK` constraint; inserting as someone else, and a blocked pair following each other, are both rejected by RLS; only the follower can unfollow (a non-follower's `DELETE` matches zero rows); a signed-in stranger reads the public follower/following counts for any account but cannot read a raw `follows` row belonging to two other people; `anon` is rejected outright on every function and gets an empty, non-erroring result from the plain-RLS `follows` table (no policy applies to `anon`, so it's excluded rather than denied — a different but equally correct shape from the function-level rejections). For `posts`: a stranger reads another account's posts via `nacti_prispevky()`; a blocked pair reads nothing; `anon` is rejected outright; only a post's own author can insert as themselves or delete it; the `posts` Storage bucket's own RLS lets the owner write to their own folder, rejects a write into someone else's, and allows a read from anyone. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing, RPC-mocked via `mockSupabase()`'s existing `rpc` option rather than a table read, since both count functions and `nacti_prispevky()` are RPCs, not plain REST reads): the stat row shows the right follower/following/post counts computed from mocked `follows`/`posts` rows; an existing post renders as a correctly-sized grid tile (the same rendering discipline that caught the Stories-phase sign-URL bug, this time proving the *public*-bucket path — `getPublicUrl()`, no signing involved — also needed a matching `/storage/v1/object/public/` route added to the shared mock, now covering both `avatary`'s and `posts`' image requests generically for any future feature that reads a public bucket); opening a tile and deleting it removes it from the grid; adding a new post through the real upload→insert flow makes it appear. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected.

**The second phase carried the same IG layout onto `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` (a stranger's profile), where the Follow button and the stat row actually mean something they can't on your own profile — and this pass moved the underlying stat/tab/grid CSS out of `ProfilModule.css` into `SocialModule.css` first, because reusing the *classes* across the two screens only works if a stylesheet guaranteed to load in both places actually defines them.** `ProfilModule.css` is only ever loaded when the app's own `/profil` page has been visited; `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` renders from inside `SocialModule.tsx`'s own lazy chunk, reachable by opening `/social` directly without ever touching `/profil` first — so a class defined only in `ProfilModule.css` would have silently rendered unstyled there. Renamed `profil-staty-*`/`profil-prispevky-*`/`profil-prispevek-*` to `social-staty-*`/`social-prispevky-*`/`social-prispevek-*` in the move, matching the file they now actually live in; `pages/profil/components/ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` (the own-profile version) updated to the same renamed classes rather than keeping two near-identical copies of the same rules in two files.

**`PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` (the full-screen single-post viewer) moved from `pages/profil/components/` into `social/components/` in the same pass, and gained a `jeMoje` prop it didn't need before** — the version built for the account's own profile could safely assume every post it would ever show belonged to the viewer, so its delete button had no ownership check at all; a stranger's profile shows *their* posts, which the viewer must never let you delete. `jeMoje` gates the trash icon the same way `StoryProhlizec.tsx` already gates its own delete button for a story that might not belong to the viewer — this component just needed to catch up to a distinction Stories already had to solve. `PridatPrispevekDialog.tsx` (posting) stayed put in `pages/profil/components/` — uploading *to* a profile only ever makes sense for your own, so a stranger's profile dialog has no use for it and never imports it.

**The Follow button lives in the same `.social-dialog-akce.social-profil-akce` row as the existing friend actions (Napsat/Přidat/Nahlásit/Blokovat), first in the row, not a separate section — because following and friending are unrelated axes in this app (a real one-way follow next to a real two-way friendship, per this session's own earlier decision), and stacking them as two different visual clusters would have implied a relationship between them that doesn't exist.** `.social-profil-akce { flex-wrap: wrap; }` (already in place from before this pass) was enough to keep a now-four-button row from overflowing on a narrow phone — nothing new needed there. Toggling calls `sledovatUcet()`/`prestatSledovatUcet()` then unconditionally refetches `nactiVztahSledovani()` rather than optimistically incrementing a local count — the server is the only real source of truth for a number nothing else in the client tracks, and the round trip is imperceptible for a single-row table. The button (and the whole stat/tab/grid block below it) is hidden only by the dialog's own existing `!jeToJa` guard on the actions row for the button itself — the stats and grid stay visible even in the (currently unreachable in practice) case of viewing your own id through this dialog, since they're still true numbers either way; only "follow yourself," which the database's own `follows_no_self` constraint would reject anyway, is actually suppressed.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing) and a full-page screenshot checked by eye: opening a friend's profile (via the existing `?kod=` deep link) shows the right name, bio, mutual-friend count, an already-correct "Sledujete" state seeded from a mocked `follows` row, the stat row, and their one mocked post rendered as a real, non-zero-sized grid tile; opening that post shows its caption with no delete button offered at all (`jeMoje={false}` from a real other-user id, not just an untested code path); tapping "Sledovat" flips it to "Sledujete" once the mocked insert round-trips. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

### Chat DM feature audit and message editing

**Asked to compare the chat feature against a checklist (text/emoji/photo/video/voice/reactions/reply/edit/delete/seen/typing/online/block/report) before building anything else, this session read the actual code rather than trusting memory — the audit found exactly one feature genuinely missing outright (editing) and one narrower-than-expected (global online status, which was already a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight, documented under Social above).** Everything else on the list already existed: text, images, video, voice messages, reactions (`👍❤️😂😮😢🙏` — missing only 🔥 from the user's example set, a one-line addition not yet made), reply, soft delete, 1:1 read receipts, typing indicator, per-chat presence, block, and report were all already shipped in earlier passes this session. Editing had no trace anywhere — no column, no RLS policy, no API function, no UI — confirmed by grepping the whole `social/` tree before starting, not assumed missing from the checklist alone.

**Editing was built to the same one-line-summary standard as deleting: no version history, just the current text plus a timestamp that flips on an "(upraveno)" tag — deliberately the same simplicity Discord/WhatsApp both settle on, not a git-style diff view nobody asked for.** `messages.edited_at timestamptz` is the only schema change; there's no separate `messages_history` table, because keeping every previous draft of every message forever is a cost with no asked-for use case, the same "why build the harder version nobody requested" restraint this file applies everywhere else.

**The RLS policy that made this possible already existed, and reusing it surfaced a small pre-existing gap that got closed in the same migration.** `messages` already had an `UPDATE` policy called "smazat vlastní zprávu" (delete your own message) — but its `USING`/`WITH CHECK` only ever tested `auth.uid() = sender_id`, with no restriction on *which* columns changed, so it was really "update any column of your own row," soft-delete just happened to be the only thing that had ever called it. Editing is the second caller of the exact same policy, not a new one — renamed to "upravit vlastní zprávu" to describe what it actually permits. Reusing it surfaced the gap explicitly: nothing had ever stopped a client from also changing `chat_id` on their own message (moving it into a chat they don't belong to, an orphaning move nobody had a reason to make before, but the policy allowed it). `WITH CHECK` gained `jsem_clenem(chat_id)` in the same migration — verified directly (moving a real message into a chat the sender isn't a member of gets rejected with `42501`) rather than left as a latent hole now that a second feature depends on the same policy being correct.

**The composer is the same UI for writing a new message and editing an old one, not a second input** — `upravujeZpravu: Zprava | null` (the whole message, not just an id, so `ulozitUpravu` has its `mediaType` on hand without a second lookup) switches the form's meaning: the attach button and the mic-vs-send swap (Pomodoro's "hide what would be a no-op" idiom, reused) both disable while editing, since attaching a new file or recording a new voice note mid-edit doesn't mean anything, and the submit button's icon/label and the input's placeholder change to say "Uložit úpravu"/"Uprav zprávu…" instead of "Odeslat"/"Napiš zprávu…". Starting a reply clears any in-progress edit and vice versa — the two banners share `.social-odpoved-lista`'s exact layout (an accent-cyan left border is the only visual difference) rather than inventing a second banner style for what's structurally the same "composer is doing something other than plain typing" state.

**Editing a media message's caption down to nothing is allowed, mirroring `poslatZpravu`'s own existing tolerance for a captionless upload** — `messages.body` still can't be empty (`CHECK length(body) >= 1`), so `upravitZpravu()` falls back to the same `VYCHOZI_POPISEK_MEDIA` placeholder a fresh captionless upload already gets, and the composer's submit button stays enabled with an empty field specifically when the message being edited carries media (`muzeUlozitPrazdny`) — a plain text message with no media still correctly can't be edited down to empty.

**Realtime needed no new wiring at all — `sledovatChat()`'s existing `event: '*'` subscription already delivered `UPDATE`s (soft-deletes) to every participant, and an edit is just another `UPDATE` on the same row.** The only reason it didn't already carry `edited_at` to other participants is that the column didn't exist yet; adding it to `zpravaZRadku`'s mapping and to both `.select(...)` column lists (`nactiZpravy`, `poslatZpravu`) was the whole client-side change needed for a saved edit to appear live on someone else's screen, with no changes to the subscription itself.

**Building this against the mock surfaced a real, previously-unneeded gap in the shared `tests/e2e/helpers.ts`: the PATCH handler always answered `[]`, because no earlier feature had ever called `.update(...).select().single()`.** `smazatZpravu()`/`ztlumitChat()`/every other existing `.update()` call in this codebase reads nothing back, so the mock never had to. `upravitZpravu()` is the first to chain `.select().single()` on an `UPDATE`, and the old mock's `[]` response made `.single()` see zero rows and report an error even though the underlying row genuinely got updated — the fix (mirroring the `POST` handler's own already-existing `jednoP` accept-header check) collects the rows a `PATCH` actually touched and returns the single object or the array depending on the same `vnd.pgrst.object` header `POST` already checks, fixed in the shared, permanent helper rather than worked around in the throwaway spec, since any future feature that reads back an `UPDATE` will need the same capability.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: the message's own sender edits it successfully; a non-sender's attempted edit is silently rejected (the row is unchanged, not merely an error); moving a message into a chat the sender isn't a member of is rejected by the tightened `WITH CHECK`. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a friend's message offers no "Upravit" option at all; the sender's own message opens the composer pre-filled with its current text, saves the new text in place (not appended as a new message), and shows the "(upraveno)" tag once saved. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected.

**Online/offline among friends is the second item off the same checklist, and it deliberately reopens a boundary the file had drawn for a *different* presence feature earlier, not the same one.** `sledovatPritomnost()` (the "who's in this chat right now" Realtime Presence channel documented under Accounts & Social above) was scoped to an open chat specifically to avoid a global "who's online" channel anyone who'd ever seen your id could watch forever. Asked explicitly whether to build that global version anyway, the answer that came back was narrower than global: visible to accepted friends only, not to a stranger who once sent a rejected friend request. That's a real, distinct scope decision — captured in a code comment on `sledovatPritomnost()` itself now, so the two features don't read as contradicting each other on a later pass.

**This app-wide presence is a plain table with a heartbeat, not a second Realtime Presence channel** — `presence(user_id primary key, last_seen_at)`, RLS scoped to `user_id = auth.uid() or (je_muj_pritel(user_id) and not je_blokovan_se_mnou(user_id))` for `SELECT`, self-row-only for `INSERT`/`UPDATE`. "Online" is never stored as a boolean; the client derives it from how fresh `last_seen_at` is (`JE_ONLINE_PRAH_MS`, roughly two missed heartbeats' worth of tolerance) — the same "absolute timestamp, not a countdown or a stored flag" idiom `registerSW.ts`'s resume logic and Pomodoro's `endsAt` already established, chosen over a real second Presence channel because a plain table with RLS is a mechanism this codebase already trusts and reuses everywhere (`friendships`, `stories`, `chat_members`), while Realtime Presence's own channel-membership model doesn't have a native way to say "visible to these specific people" — a presence channel broadcasts to whoever's subscribed to it, not to whoever a database policy says may see the tracker.

**The heartbeat itself has to run app-wide, from `App.tsx`, not from anywhere inside Social — a friend needs to see you as online while you're in the Hub or a miniapp, not only while you have Social open.** `social/presence.ts`'s `startPresence()` joins the same one-time-owner startup list as `startInbox()` (unread messages, the precedent this follows almost exactly) — ticks every 25 s while the tab is actually visible, plus once immediately on `visibilitychange` back to visible, and otherwise no-ops. Wiring an app-wide background service through `social/api.ts` was already an accepted cost by the time this landed — `startInbox()` already pulls `social/api.ts` into the eager main bundle via `App.tsx`, so `startPresence()` calling three more of its exports doesn't cross a boundary that wasn't already crossed; confirmed by the actual build output growing by only ~1.3 KB, not by a new lazy-vs-eager regression the way `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`'s UI weight would have been.

**The read side stays exactly where it was always going to: `SocialAvatar.tsx` gained one `online` boolean prop, resolved by callers, not computed inside the component** — the same "component draws what it's told, doesn't decide eligibility itself" shape `frame` already established there for avatar frames. A small corner dot (`.social-avatar-online-tecka`, positioned absolute with its own `border` matching the app background) reuses the exact "a status indicator needs a backing colour, not just a mask" lesson this session already learned twice on this exact component (the identity ring's own gap, and Stories' second ring around it) — applied here from the start rather than being a fourth bug to catch. `useOnlineFriends()` (`social/presence.ts`) is the one hook every caller (`PratelePanel.tsx`, `ChatyPanel.tsx`'s 1:1 rows, `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`) shares — it loads the friends' last-seen map once, subscribes to live changes (`presence` joined the `supabase_realtime` publication, same "RLS already gates what Realtime delivers" reasoning as everywhere else this pattern appears), and additionally re-evaluates every 10 s on a plain interval even with no new event, because a friend going stale (closing the app) produces no event at all — nothing writes a "went offline" row, so the only way to notice is to keep checking whether the last known timestamp has aged past the threshold.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: the account's own heartbeat upserts successfully; a genuine friend reads it; a non-friend and a blocked friend are both rejected (empty result, not an error, matching the plain-RLS-table shape this file's own precedent already distinguishes from a `SECURITY DEFINER` function's outright rejection); `anon` gets nothing; writing to someone else's row is rejected outright. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing) plus a screenshot checked by eye: the app's own heartbeat write is observed to actually happen on load, with no Social screen ever opened; a friend seeded with a fresh `last_seen_at` shows the corner dot on the profile page's friends list, one seeded ten minutes stale does not. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected.

**The last two items off the checklist — group read receipts and a 🔥 reaction — both turned out to need no new backend at all, just catching up client code to data that was already there.** `nactiPrectenost()`/`sledovatPrectenost()` had returned *every* member's `last_read_at` since the 1:1 read-receipt feature shipped — `ChatView.tsx` just never read past the one entry it needed for a pair. Group support is the same computation the 1:1 case already does (does a member's `last_read_at` sit at or after the sender's last message), just run over every entry in `chat.ucastnici` instead of one, then formatted as "Přečteno" when the count equals the full member list and "Přečteno *N* z *M*" otherwise (and not rendered at all when nobody's read it yet — a group chat's very first message showing "Přečteno 0 z 4" would read as noise, not information). `message_reactions.emoji` was always a plain `text` column with no `CHECK` constraint enumerating allowed values — `EMOJI_REAKCI` is a client-side-only fixed list (same "pevná sada, ne libovolný vstup" restraint as `IKONY_SKUPIN`), so adding 🔥 to it needed no migration, just the one array literal in `types.ts`.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a group chat with three members, one who's read the sender's last message and one who hasn't, shows "Přečteno 1 z 2"; once the second member's `last_read_at` catches up, it collapses to plain "Přečteno" — same text the 1:1 case has always shown, so a fully-read message reads identically regardless of chat size. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (neither change touched routing or the bundle).

**This closes every item from the DM feature checklist this session started from** (text/emoji/images/video/voice/reactions/reply/edit/delete/seen/typing/online/block/report) — editing, friends-only online status, group read receipts and the missing reaction emoji were the four real gaps found by actually reading the code rather than guessing from memory; everything else was already shipped in earlier passes.

**"Moje úspěchy"/"Moje cíle" were pulled off the real profile page right after the IG-style redesign, deliberately *for now*, not deleted as a feature.** `ProfilModule.tsx` dropped the `.profil-two-col` block (the unlocked-badges card plus `<ProfilGoals>`) and the imports/hooks that only fed it (`useAppStore`'s `setActiveAppId`, `useGoalTracker`'s `goals`, `useGamificationStore`'s `badges`) — the page now flows straight from the level/streak card into the IG stat row (Příspěvky/Sledující/Sledovaní) → tabs → grid → MŮJ KÓD, with nothing between them. `ProfilGoals.tsx` itself, and the `.profil-two-col`/`.profil-col-card`/`.profil-col-head`/`.profil-list-item`/`.profil-item-details` CSS rules that only it still uses, were deliberately left in place rather than deleted alongside — the removal is a display choice about what the newly-crowded profile page shows today, not a decision that goal-tracking or a badges shortcut don't belong there; reinstating either later is putting the same block back, not rebuilding it. `.profil-item-icon`/`.profil-item-title`/`.profil-item-sub`/`.profil-link-btn` stayed in `ProfilModule.css` unchanged for the same reason — `ProfilGoals.tsx` still renders them. Verified with `npm run typecheck` (clean — confirms nothing else still reached for the removed hooks/imports), `npm run test:unit`, `npm run build` (the main bundle shrank slightly, not grew, confirming nothing orphaned stayed reachable), the full `auth.spec.ts` regression run, and a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) screenshotting `/profil` to confirm the page reads cleanly with the gap closed.

**The settings-style menu that used to sit at the bottom of the real profile page (Osobní informace/Zabezpečení/Vzhled aplikace/Nápověda a podpora/Synchronizace/Verze aplikace) moved to `/nastaveni` — and moved, not duplicated, because three of those six rows were never anything but a link to that exact page.** `ProfilModule.tsx`'s `.profil-settings-list` was six `profil-menu-row`s, but only two of them (Synchronizace, Verze aplikace) actually *did* something on the profile page itself — the other four either just called `navigate('/nastaveni')` (Osobní informace, Zabezpečení, Vzhled aplikace) or were a `BRZY` placeholder with no real destination at all (Nápověda a podpora, superseded in practice by `SettingsModule.tsx`'s already-working "Podpora" ticket card).
