Once the whole block is meant to live in Nastavení, those three teaser rows would be pointing at a page they're already sitting on — dead weight, not something to carry over — so they're simply gone, while the two rows with real behaviour (`useCloudStatus`/`syncNow`, and `hasNewerVersion`/`applyUpdateNow`/`checkForUpdates`) became two new `settings-card` sections in `SettingsModule.tsx`, positioned right after Zabezpečení and before Podpora, reusing that file's existing card/button classes rather than inventing new ones. `.profil-cloud-dot`'s styling moved with the card it belongs to, renamed `.settings-cloud-dot` in `SettingsModule.css` — the same "a class only works where its stylesheet actually loads" reasoning `ProfilModule.css` → `SocialModule.css` already established for the stat/tab/grid CSS earlier this session, applied here in the opposite direction. `ProfilModule.tsx`'s own ⚙️ icon in the top bar, already wired to `/nastaveni`, is now the only way back to any of this from the profile page — nothing new needed there. Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (main bundle slightly smaller again) and a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) asserting none of the six old menu labels render on `/profil` any more while Synchronizace and Verze aplikace both render and work on `/nastaveni`, plus a full-page screenshot of each route checked by eye.

### Sledování/přátelství sjednocení a soukromý účet

**Asked to stop keeping "friends" (`friendships`, request/accept, mutual) and "follow" (`follows`, one-way, instant, added in the IG-redesign pass) as two parallel relationship systems, and to add a public/private account toggle — the Instagram model was the explicit reference point: public follows instantly, private queues a request the target approves, and mutual accepted follow is what "friend" now means everywhere else in the app.** This is a real reversal of the decision documented earlier in this file (`follows` was deliberately kept separate from `friendships` because "the two rows mean genuinely different things") — the reversal is the point: the app no longer wants two relationship concepts, and the earlier reasoning stops applying once a private account can require approval, which is what actually made `friendships`' request/accept step necessary in the first place.

**The whole migration leans on one lever: `je_muj_pritel(druhy)` keeps its exact name and signature, only its body changes — from "an accepted `friendships` row" to "mutual, both-sides-`stav='prijato'`, `follows` rows."** Every RLS policy and helper this app already had gating chat (`zaloz_chat`, `jsem_clenem`-adjacent checks), presence, Secret Chat eligibility, Stories visibility and 1:1 read receipts was written against `je_muj_pritel`, never against `friendships` directly — so redefining the one function is the entire security-model migration for all of those; none of their own SQL needed to change. `pocet_spolecnych_pratel`/`navrhy_pratel`/`admin_prehled`'s friendship count were the only other functions reading `friendships` directly, redefined the same way (mutual accepted `follows` instead of accepted `friendships` rows).

**`follows` gained a `stav` column (`'cekajici' | 'prijato'`), and the client never gets to set it — a `BEFORE INSERT` trigger (`nastav_stav_sledovani`) looks up the target's `profiles.soukromy` and decides, the same "server computes, client only requests" shape as `message_reactions`' `chat_id` trigger and `support_zpravy`'s `je_od_podpory` check.** A public target's row lands `'prijato'` immediately; a private target's lands `'cekajici'` until approved. Approving goes through `schvalit_sledovani(sledujici)`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function rather than a raw `UPDATE` RLS policy — a plain policy scoped to "the target can update a pending row" would have no way to also stop them from rewriting `follower_id`/`following_id` on that same row, the same class of gap this file's own `tajne_chaty` design note already flagged for exactly this reason. Declining an incoming request or removing an existing follower, by contrast, needed no function: a new plain `DELETE` policy (`following_id = auth.uid()`) is safe with no extra guard, because deleting your own inbound edge can't corrupt anything — it also doubles as a real "remove follower" capability nobody had to build separately, and as what `zablokovat()` now uses to clear the incoming edge on block (both directions of a `follows` pair, not just the caller's own — needed because `profiles`' own SELECT policy grants visibility through `je_muj_pritel`/pending-follow the same way it always granted it through `friendships`).

**`moji_pratele()` is a new `SECURITY DEFINER` function replacing `nactiPratele()`'s old direct `friendships` read — a real friend list has no single row per relationship any more (it's two independent `follows` edges), so pairing them up is server-side work, not something to redo per client.** Both new functions were checked against `anon` before shipping (this file's own now-four-times-caught mistake of a `SECURITY DEFINER` function keeping its default `PUBLIC`/`anon` grant) — this time caught immediately rather than after the fact: `get_advisors` flagged the brand-new trigger function `nastav_stav_sledovani()` itself as anon-executable via RPC (harmless in practice, since it only makes sense with `NEW` inside a real trigger context and would error on direct invocation, but revoked anyway, same discipline as everywhere else in this file) — a fifth instance of the identical class of mistake, this time on a function that isn't even meant to be called directly at all.

**Data migration went one way only, per explicit instruction: every accepted `friendships` row became a mutual, `'prijato'` pair in `follows` (both directions inserted, `on conflict` folding into whatever already existed); every pending `friendships` row was simply not carried over — a declined-by-default clean start, not an attempt to reconstruct pending follow requests that never had an equivalent shape.** `friendships` was dropped in the same migration once every reader was rewritten — kept table shells around for something with a clean, already-executed migration path is what this file's own SchoolBuddy-rename section explicitly warns against doing needlessly; the difference from that case is that `friendships`' rows genuinely fit into `follows` with no data loss, unlike an XOR key or a persisted store name that can't be migrated at all.

**Being private isn't just a follow-approval gate — it hides the profile's actual content, or the toggle would protect nothing worth protecting.** `nacti_prispevky(cil)` now also checks `not soukromy or cil = auth.uid() or je_muj_pritel(cil)`, so a private account's post grid is empty to anyone who isn't the owner or an accepted mutual follow — Stories inherited this for free (their own SELECT policy already gated on `je_muj_pritel`, so redefining that one function silently tightened Stories' visibility the same way, no Stories-specific change needed). `precti_verejny_profil()` deliberately does **not** hide the rest of the profile (name, bio, level, streak, role) behind privacy — matching Instagram's own behavior of showing identity even for a private account, only content and follower/following *lists* (never counts) staying gated; `pocet_sledujicich`/`pocet_sledovanych` stay public numbers regardless.

**Every place that used to show two separate relationship actions (a "Sledovat/Sledujete" follow button plus a "Přidat/Žádost odeslána/Napsat" friend-request button) now shows one.** `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`'s single button reads `VztahSledovani.stavSledovani` (`'nesleduje' | 'cekajici' | 'prijato'`, replacing the old plain `sledujiHo` boolean) and labels itself `Sledovat`/`Požádat o sledování` (soukromý cíl)/`Čeká na schválení`/`Sledujete`; clicking it while `cekajici` just unfollows — the exact same "delete your own outbound edge" call cancels a still-pending request, no separate "cancel" action needed. "Napsat" only appears once `stav.pratele` actually contains the other person (mutual, not just followed). A locked account additionally shows a `🔒 Soukromý účet` tag and, if the viewer isn't the owner or a mutual friend, a lock message in place of the post grid instead of a misleading "Zatím žádné fotky." — the server already returns nothing there either way, the client message just explains why. `VyhledavacPanel.tsx`'s search/suggestion rows lost their "Přidat" button in favor of "Sledovat" (calling the same `sledovatUcet` the profile dialog uses, showing "Sleduješ ✓" or "Žádost o sledování odeslána." depending on what the insert's trigger-computed `stav` came back as), and its "ŽÁDOSTI"/"ČEKÁ NA ODPOVĚĎ" sections became "ŽÁDOSTI O SLEDOVÁNÍ"/"ČEKÁ NA SCHVÁLENÍ" — same shape (`Zadost.smer`), now backed by `follows` rows with `stav = 'cekajici'` instead of `friendships` rows with `status = 'pending'`, and only ever populated at all when at least one side involved is private.

**`Pritel`/`Zadost` (`types.ts`) both lost their old row-`id` field — there is no single-row identity for either concept any more.** `Pritel` is just `{ profil }` (a mutual pair, two independent edges, nothing to key an "unfriend" action off besides the other person's own id); `Zadost` is `{ profil, smer, createdAt }`, and accept/decline/cancel all take the other account's id directly (`schvalitZadost`/`zrusitVazbu`), not a row id — the three call sites that used to key a React list off `p.vazbaId` (`PratelePanel.tsx`, `ChatyPanel.tsx`'s group-invite picker, `SpravaSkupinyDialog.tsx`'s add-member list) all switched to `p.profil.id`, which is unique across a friend list by construction anyway.

**"Soukromý účet" lives in `/nastaveni`'s own new Soukromí card, a plain switch next to Zabezpečení — not in the local, `secureStorage`-backed `useProfileData` profile, because privacy is inherently a cloud/Social concept with no offline meaning.** `social/api.ts` gained `nactiSoukromy`/`nastavSoukromy`, both plain reads/writes of `profiles.soukromy` on the caller's own row (the existing self-row `UPDATE` policy on `profiles` already covers it, same as name/motto/bio — no new policy needed), and `SettingsModule.tsx` loads the value once on mount the same way `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` loads a stranger's profile, rather than folding it into the store that already tracks the theme/frame/bio fields cloud sync mirrors.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation, the three-shape check this file applies to every new relationship surface**: following a public account lands `'prijato'` immediately, following a private one lands `'cekajici'`; `je_muj_pritel` is false before approval and true only once both sides hold an accepted edge; `moji_pratele()` returns exactly the mutual set; `nacti_prispevky()` returns nothing for a stranger against a private account and the real rows for a mutual friend; declining a pending request removes exactly that row; `anon` is rejected outright on `schvalit_sledovani`/`moji_pratele` (and, once caught, on the trigger function itself). **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): the Soukromí switch flips and toasts correctly; a search result search seeded with mixed pending directions renders "ŽÁDOSTI O SLEDOVÁNÍ"/"ČEKÁ NA SCHVÁLENÍ" with the right people in each; a friend seeded via the mocked `moji_pratele` RPC shows up on the real profile page's friends list; opening a private stranger's profile shows the 🔒 tag, "Požádat o sledování," and the lock message instead of the post grid. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Deliberately not built in the first pass, flagged rather than assumed included: the "message requests" DM behavior discussed alongside this unification.** A follow-up pass built exactly what was flagged — mutual follow keeps opening a normal chat exactly as `je_muj_pritel`-gated chat creation always has, but a 1:1 chat with anyone else non-blocked now lands as a request instead of being refused outright, matching the Instagram behavior this was compared against.

**The whole feature is one column, one trigger and one relaxed check — no new table, because `chat_members` already had exactly the per-member state a request needs.** `chat_members` gained `stav` (`'pozadavek' | 'prijato'`), the same two-value shape as `follows.stav` above; `zaloz_chat()`'s per-participant loop dropped its `je_muj_pritel` requirement for a 1:1 chat (a group still requires every invitee to already be a friend — group message requests aren't in scope, and inviting someone who's still deciding whether to talk to you at all into a group makes no sense) and instead computes each invited member's starting `stav` inline: `'prijato'` for a friend or a group, `'pozadavek'` otherwise. The creator's own row is always `'prijato'` — founding the chat is itself their consent, the same "acting party is trusted, the invited party is what needs a gate" shape `zaloz_tajny_chat`/`schvalit_sledovani` above already use.

**Replying is itself acceptance, and that's the whole mechanism — no separate "accept" write path needed for the common case.** `prijmi_pozadavek_po_zprave()`, an `AFTER INSERT` trigger on `messages`, flips the *sender's own* `chat_members.stav` from `'pozadavek'` to `'prijato'` whenever they send into a chat where it's still pending — the same "the reaction itself is the signal" shape `oznac_ticket_po_zprave()` already uses for reopening a support ticket. This means a message-request recipient who just types back never has to find an explicit accept button at all. One is still there anyway (`prijmoutPozadavekNaZpravu()`, a plain self-row `UPDATE` — no new RLS policy, the existing unrestricted self-row `UPDATE` policy on `chat_members` already covers it) for someone who wants to signal acceptance without necessarily replying yet.

**Declining a request needed no new mechanism at all — it's the exact same action as leaving a chat.** `opustitChat()` was already a plain self-row `DELETE` from `chat_members`; a pending request and an active chat both disappear from your own view the same way, so "✕" on a request row and "Opustit chat" in an open chat's header call the identical function. Re-requesting after a decline works for free too, the same reason a rejected Secret Chat invite could be re-sent: `zaloz_chat`'s existing chat-reuse lookup joins on the invited party's current `chat_members` row, which a decline just deleted — nothing to find, so a fresh `zaloz_chat` call makes a brand-new chat rather than resurrecting the declined one.

**`otevritChatSPritelem()` — the function `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`'s "Napsat" button and `PratelePanel.tsx` called — is renamed `otevritChat()`, because "s přítelem" stopped being true the moment a non-friend could call it too.** The dialog's "Napsat" button itself, previously shown only when `pritel` (mutual friend) was true, now shows for anyone not blocked — clicking it either opens the existing conversation, resumes a pending request, or starts a brand-new one, and `zaloz_chat` on the other end decides which without the client needing to know in advance which case it is.

**`ChatyPanel.tsx` splits `stav.chaty` into two client-side groups by `pozadavek`, not two separate loads — the data was always one query, only the rendering needed to change.** Pending requests get their own "POŽADAVKY NA ZPRÁVY" card above the normal "CHATY" list, same accept/decline icon-button shape `VyhledavacPanel.tsx`'s follow-request rows already established. Opening a still-pending chat (`ChatView.tsx`) shows a small gold banner — a deliberately different color from the reply/edit banner's cyan, since this is a state of the whole chat, not an action on one message — with its own "Přijmout" button, right below the header and above the message list. The global unread badge (`inbox.ts`) was deliberately left untouched: a request chat's unread messages still count toward it exactly like any other chat's, on the reasoning that a genuine message deserves to be noticed same as any other, not muted by default the way `mujMuted` chats are — only the *list position* changes, not whether it can reach you.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a 1:1 chat between two accounts with no follow relationship at all succeeds and lands the invited party at `'pozadavek'`, the founder at `'prijato'`; the invited party replying flips their own row to `'prijato'`; a *group* chat with the same non-friend pair is still rejected with the existing "Do skupiny jde přidat jen přítele" error; a chat with a blocked account is still rejected exactly as before. `prijmi_pozadavek_po_zprave()` was caught anon-executable via RPC before shipping (the now sixth instance of this file's own recurring `SECURITY DEFINER`-grant mistake) and revoked immediately. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a mocked pending chat renders under "POŽADAVKY NA ZPRÁVY," a mocked accepted chat renders under "CHATY," opening the pending one shows the gold banner and "Přijmout" button, and a stranger's public profile (no follow relationship, not blocked) offers "Napsat" where it used to be hidden entirely. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

### Lajky a komentáře u příspěvků

**Posts had zero engagement since the IG redesign shipped — a grid with no feedback loop — and this was the explicitly agreed next step.** `post_likes` (`post_id`, `user_id`, composite PK — a plain toggle, not an emoji picker like message reactions) and `post_comments` (`id`, `post_id`, `user_id`, `text`, no edit — same "delete only, no version history" simplicity as reactions and edited messages before it) are both new tables, deliberately plain-RLS rather than `SECURITY DEFINER` functions like `nacti_prispevky` — a like or comment on a post the viewer can already see is meant to be fully visible (who, when, what), the same openness `message_reactions` already has; the client today renders only a count and "did I already like this," but the full liker list is sitting there in the table for free if a future pass wants to show it.

**Visibility had to be pulled into one shared function, `smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele(cil)`, rather than copy-pasted across three RLS surfaces.** It's exactly the check `nacti_prispevky()` already had inline (not blocked, and — for a private account — owner or mutual friend) — redefining `nacti_prispevky()` to call it and reusing it in the `post_likes`/`post_comments` policies means a private account's likes and comments are hidden from a stranger the instant its posts already are, with no separate rule to keep in sync.

**A real bug shipped in the first migration and was caught by the impersonation test, not by `get_advisors`: the RLS policies referenced `posts` directly in an `exists (select 1 from posts p where …)`, and `posts` has no plain `SELECT` policy at all — reads only ever went through `nacti_prispevky()`, a `SECURITY DEFINER` function.** An `exists` clause embedded in another table's policy runs with the *querying role's own* privileges, not the defining function's — so that subquery silently returned zero rows for everyone, including a post's own owner trying to comment on their own picture, and every like/comment insert was rejected outright despite the visibility logic itself being correct. Fixed by wrapping the check in two more `SECURITY DEFINER` functions, `smim_videt_prispevek(prispevek_id)` and `autor_prispevku(prispevek_id)` (needed separately for "the post's owner may delete any comment on it"), so the elevated read of `posts` happens inside a function boundary instead of a raw cross-table subquery — the general lesson, not just a one-off patch: an RLS policy that needs to peek at a *different* table only gets the definer's elevated access if that peek itself goes through a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, never through a plain subquery no matter how the surrounding policy is defined.

**Deleting a comment allows two different people for two different reasons, mirroring how this app already treats a chat message versus a chat itself.** A comment's own author can always remove it (their own words); a post's owner can also remove *any* comment on their post (moderating their own wall), the same "delete your own content, or moderate what's attached to yours" split `SpravaSkupinyDialog.tsx`'s member removal and `SocialReportPanel.tsx`'s message deletion already established elsewhere in Social — no new pattern, just the same one applied to a third surface.

**`PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` — the single full-screen post viewer shared between the account's own profile and a stranger's (`VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`) — is where both landed, since it's already the one place a post's detail is shown.** It fetches `VztahKPrispevku`/`Komentar[]` only once opened, the same "on demand, not for the whole grid" discipline `nactiVztahSledovani`/`nactiPocetSpolecnychPratel` already use for a profile dialog. Liking replays the same "always re-fetch from the server after an action, never optimistically bump a local number" idiom `prepnoutSledovani` established for follow state. A new `mujId` prop decides whose "✕" delete button shows on which comment (a comment's own author, or — regardless of authorship — the post's owner) without a second round trip, since `jeMoje` (already a prop) already answers "is this my post" for that second case. Two new `SocialIcon` cases, `heart`/`heart-filled`, follow the set's existing stroke-outline style for the unliked state and switch to a filled fuchsia shape for the liked one — the same "filled = active" signal a checkmark already carries elsewhere in this file, not a new visual language.

**Building this against the mock surfaced a real, previously-latent bug in the shared `tests/e2e/helpers.ts` POST handler, not specific to comments — any first insert into a table that already had a seeded sibling row, without the client supplying its own `id`, would have hit it.** The handler pre-fills `id`/`created_at` with generated defaults, then loops over every column a sibling row has and nulls out any the client's insert body didn't include — but the loop didn't check whether `zaklad` (the just-built defaults object) already had that column, so it stomped its own freshly-generated `id`/`created_at` back to `null` the moment "id" and "created_at" were among the sibling columns and the client hadn't sent them, which every insert here never does. It went unnoticed until now because most earlier POST-testing scenarios seeded an *empty* array for the table being inserted into (so `znameSloupce` was empty and the loop was a no-op) — a comment added on top of one already-seeded comment was the first case to actually exercise it. Fixed with one added condition (`&& !(sloupec in zaklad)`) in the shared, permanent helper, not worked around in the throwaway spec, since any future feature inserting into a non-empty seeded table needs the same fix.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation, the three-shape check this file applies to every new relationship surface**: liking/commenting on a private stranger's post is rejected before the two accounts are mutual friends, and succeeds immediately after; a non-friend, non-owner account is rejected on both a like and a comment against that same post and sees zero comments when reading; the post's owner can delete a comment they didn't write, a comment's own author can delete their own; `anon` is rejected outright on `smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele` and on a direct table read of `post_likes` (the policy's own function call denies it before RLS even gets to filter rows). **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a mocked existing like and comment render with the right count and author; adding a like flips the heart to filled and bumps the count; posting a new comment appends it in the correct (oldest-first) order once the mock helper bug above was fixed. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

### Feed na Domů

**Domů had been a Stories bar and a stub card ("Sem brzy přibude víc") since the bottom-nav rework — with likes/comments now shipped, a feed was the obvious next thing to put there, and the explicit brief was "something TikTok-like, but Buddy's own, not a copy."** The result keeps TikTok's actual interaction model (one post fills the available height, vertical swipe/scroll-snap between posts, actions as a floating column) but not its visual language — the action buttons sit inside the same frosted glass circles (`.social-icon-btn`'s look, reused as `.social-feed-akce-kruh`) every other icon button in Social already uses, not TikTok's bare drop-shadowed icons, and the liked-heart color and "filled = active" convention are the same ones this session already established for post likes elsewhere, not a new one invented for this screen alone.

**Whose posts appear was a real product decision, not an implementation detail — resolved as "everyone you follow, not just mutual friends," matching Instagram's own feed rather than narrowing to the app's "friend" concept.** `nacti_feed(kurzor, pocet)` unions the caller's own posts with everyone they have an accepted (`stav = 'prijato'`) `follows` row toward, then applies the same `smi_videt_prispevky_uzivatele()` gate every other post-reading surface already uses. That gate is stricter than "I follow them" for a private account specifically — an approved one-way follow of a private account doesn't unlock their posts unless the follow is *also* mutual, the same restriction `nacti_prispevky()` already had — so the feed doesn't add a new privacy rule, it just inherits whatever was already decided there. Own posts appear in the feed too, deliberately (confirmed explicitly rather than assumed): a feed that hid your own contributions would read as an oversight, not a design choice.

**Pagination is cursor-based (`kurzor` = the oldest loaded post's `createdAt`), but unlike `nactiZpravy`'s "Načíst starší zprávy" button, loading here is triggered by scroll position, not a tap — a swipeable feed has nothing that reads as a natural place for a button.** `DomuPanel.tsx` runs a single `IntersectionObserver` over every rendered post section doing double duty: it decides which post is "active" (≥60% visible, `threshold: 0.6`) for video autoplay, and in the same callback, checks whether that active post is within the last two loaded — if so, fetches the next page. One observer instead of two (an active-post tracker plus a separate load-more sentinel) because both questions ("which post is the user looking at" and "are they close to running out") turn out to be answerable from the identical signal.

**Video autoplay is gated on that same "active" flag, not on the video simply being on screen — a `scroll-snap` feed can briefly show two posts mid-swipe, and only the one actually being viewed should have a live video.** `FeedPrispevek.tsx` plays its own `<video>` (muted, looped) when `aktivni` turns true and pauses immediately when it turns false, mirroring the resource-lifecycle discipline this file already requires elsewhere (`usePoseEngine.ts`'s camera, `TajnyChatView.tsx`'s mic) even though a paused background video is a far smaller cost than an open camera — the same principle, applied at a smaller scale.

**`FeedPrispevek` is `forwardRef`, the first place in `social/` that needed to be — `DomuPanel.tsx`'s `IntersectionObserver` needs a real DOM node per post, and there was no other way to hand one back from a component instance.** The ref attaches to the post's own root `<article>` (which also carries `data-post-id`, read back inside the observer's callback to know which post just became active); `postElementy` (a plain `Map`, not `useState`) holds the current set of DOM nodes outside React's render cycle, since which elements exist changes on every scroll-driven page load but never needs to trigger a re-render on its own — only the derived `aktivniId` state does that.

**Comments still open the exact same `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` used everywhere else — the feed doesn't get a second, TikTok-style comment tray, on purpose.** A bottom-sheet comment tray sliding up over the still-visible video is the more literally TikTok-native pattern, but it would have meant a second comment UI to keep in sync with the one `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` already has (list, author avatars, delete permissions, the input form) for a difference that's cosmetic, not functional — tapping the caption or the comment icon opens the full post view, same as tapping a grid tile on a profile does, one component doing the job everywhere it's needed rather than a near-duplicate for this one screen.

**`social/api.ts`'s `nactiProfily` (batch id → profile lookup) is exported for the first time — the feed is the first caller from outside the file itself.** Every other author-lookup use (chat member names, friend lists, comment authors) happened to stay internal to `api.ts`; `DomuPanel.tsx` needing the same batch lookup for a scrolling list of post authors was the first case where a caller outside this module needed it directly, so the function moved from a private helper to an exported one rather than gaining a second, duplicate implementation.

**`DomuPanel.tsx` needed its own layout escape from `.social-page`'s normal behavior — every other tab is content that grows and lets the *page* scroll, but a swipeable feed has to own its scrolling itself, in a fixed-height box.** `.social-panel--domu` makes the panel `flex: 1; min-height: 0` inside `.social-page`'s own flex column (sharing space with the fixed-height top bar and bottom nav exactly the way `Explorace3D`'s 3D canvas or `ChatView.tsx` already claim their own space in a flex layout elsewhere in this app) rather than growing to whatever height its content wants; `.social-feed` is the one actual scroll container inside it, with `scroll-snap-type: y mandatory` and each `.social-feed-post` sized to `height: 100%` of that box, so one post always exactly fills the available space rather than the container needing a magic-number height calculated from the viewport.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: an account seeing its own post and a followed public account's post in the feed, in the right (newest-first) order; a private account's post excluded from the feed while only a *pending* (not yet mutually accepted) follow exists toward it; a post from an account not followed at all excluded entirely; `anon` rejected outright on `nacti_feed`. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing, with screenshots checked by eye): two mocked posts render as full-height feed pages; liking the first bumps its own count and fills its own heart without affecting the second post's independent like state; scrolling reveals the second post via the real browser's native scroll-snap, confirming the CSS behaves as intended rather than merely existing in the stylesheet. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Reporting a post was a real moderation gap — neither the profile grid's `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` nor the new feed had any way to flag a post at all, unlike every other piece of Social content (messages, friends) which already went through `NahlasitDialog.tsx`.** The fix is additive to that existing pipeline, not a parallel one: `reports` gained a nullable `post_id` column alongside its existing `message_id`, and `nahlasit()` in `social/api.ts` grew one more optional parameter rather than a second reporting function — the same dialog, the same reasons list, the same "report and optionally block" flow messages already use. `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s header used to render only a trash icon, and only ever for the viewer's own post (`jeMoje` was a given everywhere it was actually reachable at the time it shipped — the profile grid only ever showed your own posts, and the public-profile phase gave it a `jeMoje` prop but never wired an alternative for the false case). The feed and a stranger's profile dialog both open this exact component on *someone else's* post, so the missing branch was a real, reachable gap, not a hypothetical one: the header now shows "Nahlásit příspěvek" whenever `jeMoje` is false, opening `NahlasitDialog` with the post's id and no `zpravaId` — mirroring how `PratelePanel.tsx` already opens the same dialog with no `zpravaId` for a friend reported outside any specific message.

**Reading a reported post as a moderator needed a new, narrow `SELECT` policy on `posts` — the same reasoning `SocialReport`'s message-reading already established for `messages`, applied here for the first time because `posts` had never had a moderator-facing read path at all.** `posts` carries no general `SELECT` policy — every ordinary read goes through `nacti_prispevky()`, deliberately, so that function alone can enforce blocking/privacy (see the Fáze 2/3 write-up above). A moderator reviewing a report is a different case: they need to see the *specific* reported post regardless of whether they follow its author, the same way `jsem_moderator()` already widens `messages`'/`profiles`' own policies "just far enough to show the reported message and the two names involved." The new policy is exactly that shape — `jsem_moderator() and exists (select 1 from reports r where r.post_id = posts.id)` — visible only for a post somebody has actually reported, nothing wider.

**Deleting a reported post goes through `moderator_smaz_prispevek(post_id)`, not a raw `.delete()`, for the same reason every other moderator-facing mutation in this file does: `posts` has no `DELETE` policy for anyone but its own author, and widening one to cover moderators would be a second, harder-to-reason-about door into the table.** The function re-checks `jsem_moderator()` itself, refuses to run on a post with no report against it (so it can't become a generic "delete anyone's post" backdoor even in a moderator's own hands), and — unlike a message's existing soft-delete — removes the row hard, matching how `posts` already has no soft-delete concept anywhere else in the app (see the Fáze 2/3 write-up: "nothing else in the schema references a post's id, so there's no conversational context a soft-delete would need to preserve"). It logs to `audit_log` via the existing `zapis_audit_log()` helper, targeting the post's own author — pattern (a) from the three-way audit-logging split documented under **Admin panel** above, one more `SECURITY DEFINER` RPC ending in the same one line every privileged RPC in this file already ends in.

**This is the RLS-subquery-elevation bug (documented for likes/comments above) resurfacing in a different shape, and it clarified the lesson rather than repeating the mistake blind.** The new `posts` `SELECT` policy's own `exists (select 1 from reports r where ...)` subquery runs with the *querying* role's privileges, same as any RLS subquery — but `reports` itself already has a working `jsem_moderator()`-gated `SELECT` policy from the original SocialReport feature, so a moderator's own query can read `reports` directly with no elevation needed; only a policy that needs to peek at a table with **no** applicable plain-RLS access for the querying role (like the earlier `post_likes`/`post_comments` reading `posts`) needs the `SECURITY DEFINER`-wrapper workaround. Verified directly rather than assumed safe by analogy: the impersonation suite confirmed a moderator's query genuinely returns the reported post row, not silently nothing.

**`nactiHlaseni()` now resolves a post-backed report's media the same way `nactiZpravy()` already resolves a chat photo — a signed/public URL fetched once at read time, not stored.** `Hlaseni` gained `postId`/`postMediaUrl`/`postMediaType`; the function batch-fetches every reported post's `media_path`/`media_type` in one `.in('id', postIds)` query (mirroring the existing batch author-lookup already in this function) and resolves each through `getPublicUrl()` — the `posts` bucket is public (see the Fáze 2/3 write-up), so this needed no signing step the way `chat-media`'s private bucket would have. A report whose post was already deleted (by its author, independent of this feature, or by a moderator's own earlier action) comes back with `postMediaUrl: null`; `SocialReportPanel.tsx` shows "Nahlášený příspěvek už neexistuje." in that case instead of a broken image, the same "report the real thing" instinct CLAUDE.md already states for this panel.

**`SocialReportPanel.tsx` renders the reported post as a small thumbnail (image or muted `<video>`) with its own delete button, a sibling block to the existing "reported message" row, not a replacement for it — a single report can only ever carry one or the other (`post_id`/`message_id` are mutually exclusive at the point `nahlasit()` is called), so the two blocks never render for the same report.** `.admin-report-prispevek-nahled` is a fixed 56×56 `object-fit: cover` box, matching the scale of the existing `admin-report-*` UI rather than a full-size preview — a moderator needs to recognize the content, not review it in detail, the same restraint the message-preview row already established with a text snippet instead of a full transcript.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a moderator reads a reported post's row via the new policy while an unreported post from the same author stays invisible to them; `moderator_smaz_prispevek` succeeds for a genuinely reported post and writes the expected `audit_log` row (confirmed by re-querying with RLS bypassed after the impersonated session, since `audit_log` itself is `jsem_admin()`-gated and a `moderator` role can't read its own write back); the same function raises rather than deletes when called against a post with no report against it; `anon`/non-moderator callers are rejected outright. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): reporting a post from a stranger's profile shows "Nahlásit příspěvek" (not a delete option) and produces the expected toast; a moderator reaching the Moderace panel (via `/nastaveni`'s "Otevřít moderaci" card, not a direct `/admin` visit — see the note on `page.goto()` restarting async role sync below) sees the reported post's thumbnail and deleting it produces "Příspěvek smazán." `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**A real Playwright timing trap surfaced while writing this feature's own verification spec, worth recording since it will recur for any future admin-panel e2e test that needs to reach a role-gated screen directly.** `page.goto('/admin')` performs a full page reload, which remounts the entire app from scratch — including the async `nactiRoliZeServeru()` role fetch `App.tsx` kicks off on every fresh mount. `App.tsx`'s own route guard evaluates against whatever role is in memory *at that instant*, which on a fresh reload is still the default `'user'` until that fetch resolves — so a direct `goto('/admin')` reliably raced the guard and bounced to `/nastaveni` before the moderator role ever loaded, even though the exact same session had already loaded that role correctly on an earlier page visit. The fix has nothing to do with the app's own code (this is a test-environment race, not a product bug) — navigate to a page where the gated content is offered but not route-guarded (`/nastaveni`'s conditional "Otevřít moderaci"/"Administrace" card), give the async role fetch time to resolve, then reach the actual gated screen via an in-app click rather than a second `goto()` — a client-side navigation that doesn't restart role sync from zero.

**Likes and comments on a post were silent — the bell (`ProfilNotifications.tsx`) never told a post's author that anyone had reacted at all, unlike every other kind of activity it already surfaces (admin broadcasts, badges, streak, tasks).** Not a reuse of `oznameni` (that table's `INSERT` policy is deliberately `jsem_admin()`-only — see **Admin panel** above — widening it to let any user broadcast into it would be a real hole, not a shortcut) and not a reuse of `social/api.ts`'s notification plumbing either, on purpose: `ProfilNotifications.tsx` is part of the app's eagerly-loaded main bundle (`ProfilModule`/`AppModule` aren't behind `React.lazy`), and pulling in Social's own Supabase-heavy `api.ts` for two small queries would have repeated the exact bundle-weight mistake the ambient-scene and `PratelSekce`/`ProfilSocialniSekce` lazy-loading fixes earlier in this file both exist to avoid. Instead it's a second small table read through `core/notifications/api.ts` — the same "one small file, one table, no Social baggage" shape that module already had for `oznameni`.

**`aktivita_prispevku` is a new, genuinely small table (`user_id` recipient, `typ` `'lajk'|'komentar'`, `od_uzivatele_id` actor, `post_id`, `precteno`, `created_at`), populated only by two `AFTER INSERT` triggers on `post_likes`/`post_comments` — the client has no `INSERT` policy on it at all, the same "only a trigger writes here" shape `zaznamenej_odpoved_podpory()` already established for support-ticket audit rows.** Both triggers look the recipient up via `autor_prispevku(post_id)` — the exact `SECURITY DEFINER` helper the report-a-post feature above just added, reused rather than re-derived — and skip the insert outright when the actor is the post's own owner, so liking or commenting on your own post never notifies you. Reading is self-row RLS (`user_id = auth.uid()`), plus a plain self-row `UPDATE`/`DELETE` policy mirroring `chat_members`' `last_read_at`/muted-flag shape, though the client doesn't actually call either today — "read" state for this item, like every other bell item, is the existing purely local `profile.readNotifications` id-list, not the DB `precteno` column; the column exists for a possible future cross-device read state, not because anything reads it yet.

**A liker or commenter on a *public* account's post isn't necessarily the recipient's friend — `nacti_prispevky()` only restricts a *private* account's posts to mutual friends, so a stranger can legitimately react to a public post — which means the recipient's own `profiles` `SELECT` policy (friends/chat-co-members only) can't be trusted to resolve that stranger's display name.** Same shape as `precti_verejny_profil()`: `nacti_aktivitu_prispevku(limit)` is a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, internally filtered to `user_id = auth.uid()` (never trusting a client-supplied recipient id) and joined against `profiles` with its own elevated read, returning the actor's name alongside each row — the client's `core/notifications/api.ts` calls this RPC, never queries `aktivita_prispevku` directly. Checked against `anon` before shipping, per this file's own now-repeated discipline: the function raises outright for a null `auth.uid()` (not silently empty), and both trigger functions have their default `PUBLIC`/`authenticated` execute grant explicitly revoked — trigger functions fire regardless of a caller's own `EXECUTE` grant, so revoking it only closes the "callable directly over `/rest/v1/rpc`" door, not the trigger's own ability to run.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a stranger liking and commenting on an account's post creates exactly two rows addressed to the post's owner, readable only by that owner (not by the stranger, not by `anon`); liking your own post creates nothing; `nacti_aktivitu_prispevku()` resolves the stranger's real display name for the recipient even with no friendship between them, rejects `anon` outright, and isn't itself callable via `anon` RPC; neither trigger function is callable via RPC by `anon` or `authenticated`. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a mocked like and a mocked comment both render as distinct bell items with the right actor name and emoji, the unread dot shows before a click and disappears after, matching the existing local read-tracking every other bell item already uses. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Double-tap-to-like was the natural companion to the feed, and it needed no new backend at all — `pridatLajk()` was already idempotent** (its own comment: "dvojí klepnutí narazí na kolizi, ne na skutečnou chybu"), which is exactly the property a double-tap gesture needs: tapping twice on an already-liked post must never unlike it, matching Instagram's own one-directional behavior, and the existing insert-or-no-op call is already safe to fire without first checking local state. `social/useDoubleTapLike.ts` is a small shared hook, not two copies of the same gesture logic in `FeedPrispevek.tsx` and `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` — both need the identical "was that second click within the window" timing and the identical heart-burst visual, and a second copy would only ever be the first one's decay target once someone changed the timing constant in only one place.

**A double-tap has to be told apart from an ordinary single tap using plain click timestamps, not the browser's native `dblclick` event — this app runs as a touch-first PWA, and relying on `dblclick` alone would have meant trusting every mobile browser to synthesize it identically from two fast taps, which isn't guaranteed the way it is for a mouse.** `zpracovatKliknuti()` compares `Date.now()` against the previous click's timestamp against a fixed 300 ms window (`OKNO_DVOJKLIKU_MS`) — under it, always treat as a like (never a toggle) and show the heart; at or over it, remember the click as a fresh "first tap." The genuinely new cost this adds is on the *single*-click path where one exists: `FeedPrispevek.tsx`'s media tap already opened the full post detail on a single click, and that action can't fire immediately any more — the hook has to wait out the same 300 ms window to make sure a second tap isn't coming before it's safe to call `onOtevritDetail`. `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s own image tap did nothing before this feature, so it passes no `onJedenKlik` at all and pays none of that delay — the hook's optional third argument exists specifically so a caller with nothing riding on the single-tap path doesn't have to.

**The heart-burst itself (`.social-feed-dvojklik-srdce`, a `SocialIcon name="heart-filled"` at 90px, centered and fading via one shared `@keyframes`) is genuinely shared CSS, not per-screen duplicated styling** — both `FeedPrispevek.tsx`'s full-height feed post and `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s `.social-story-plocha` were already `position: relative` for their own existing overlays (the feed's veil/info/actions, the story area's own centering), so the burst slots in as one more absolutely-positioned child with no layout changes needed in either. Added to the existing `prefers-reduced-motion` block alongside every other looping/one-shot animation in this file — with the animation disabled, the heart still renders at its fully-visible end state for the same duration the `window.setTimeout` in the hook already keeps it mounted, so a reduced-motion user still gets confirmation a like happened, just without the pop.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a real `dblclick()` on a feed post's image shows the heart overlay and flips the like button to its filled/`je-lajknuto` state with count `1`; a plain single click on that same image still opens the full post detail (confirming the added wait didn't break the pre-existing single-tap behavior, just delayed it by the gesture-detection window); a double-click inside the already-open `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` view also shows the heart and likes, with no competing single-click action to delay. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**A real followers/following list, and a remove-follower button, were both flagged as gaps in the same pass — only counts were ever public, and the RLS to remove a follower already existed with no UI calling it.** `nactiVztahSledovani()`'s own comment already stated the deliberate boundary this session's follow/friend unification chose: "Seznam sledujících/sledovaných appka nikdy nečte přímo (RLS na follows to ani nedovolí pro cizí dvojici)" — a *stranger's* follower/following list stays unbuilt on purpose, matching `pocet_spolecnych_pratel()`'s own "count is public, the list behind it never is" restraint. What was actually missing was narrower and safe to add: the account's own two lists, which the `follows` table's plain `SELECT` policy (`follower_id = auth.uid() or following_id = auth.uid()`) already lets the owner read directly — nothing new needed there. What did need a `SECURITY DEFINER` function, the same shape as `nacti_aktivitu_prispevku()` just above, is resolving each row's *display name*: `profiles`' own SELECT policy only grants visibility through `je_muj_pritel()` (mutual) or a **pending** follow, never an accepted one-way follow, so a plain client-side profile lookup would come back empty for exactly the accounts a one-way follow list exists to show. `moji_sledujici()`/`moje_sledovani()` join `follows` to `profiles` internally, filtered to `auth.uid()`'s own edges only — never a parameterized "whose list," so there's no way to widen this into the very stranger-list exposure the codebase has twice already declined to build.

**Removing a follower needed no new backend at all — `SpravaSkupinyDialog.tsx`'s "member removal has no safe plain-RLS shape" caveat doesn't apply here, because unlike a group chat this is a two-party relationship the target already has standing authority over.** The migration that unified follow/friends already added "odebrat sledujícího nebo odmítnout žádost smí jen cíl" (`following_id = auth.uid()`) specifically so a private account's pending-request decline and an already-accepted follower's removal could share one policy — `odebratSledujiciho()` in `api.ts` is a plain `.delete()` against it, the first caller that policy ever had.

**`SledujiciDialog.tsx` follows `NahlasitDialog.tsx`'s dialog shape (`.social-overlay` + `.social-dialog` as siblings, no `createPortal`), not `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s full-screen-viewer shape** — this is a small, centered, dismissible list, the same class of UI `NahlasitDialog` already is, not a screen-filling surface that risks the stacking-context trap documented for Stories/post viewers. It opens from the account's own "Sledující"/"Sledovaní" stat cells in `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` — those two cells switched from a plain `<div>` to a reset-styled `<button>` (`.social-staty-cislo--klikatelne`, visually identical to before), while the "Příspěvky" cell next to them stays inert, since scrolling to the grid already serves that count's purpose. Selecting a row closes this dialog and opens the existing `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` on that person, the same "one shared profile dialog, reached from every list" pattern `PratelePanel.tsx`/`VyhledavacPanel.tsx` already established — no new profile view built for this entry point. The "Sledovaní" tab also offers "Přestat sledovat" (reusing the pre-existing `prestatSledovatUcet()`) alongside the "Sledující" tab's new "Odebrat" — a view-only list with no action on either tab would have read as unfinished next to every other list in this codebase, which all already let you act on a row.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: an account genuinely following another (accepted, non-mutual) shows up correctly in the follower's `moji_sledujici()` and the followed account's own `moje_sledovani()`; removing a follower via the existing `following_id = auth.uid()` policy deletes exactly that row; `anon` is rejected outright on both functions (not silently empty) and isn't itself able to call either via RPC. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): opening "Sledující" shows a mocked follower and "Odebrat" removes them from the list (falling back to the empty-state copy); switching to "Sledovaní" shows a mocked followed account and "Přestat sledovat" removes them the same way. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**"Uloženo" had been a stub since the IG redesign shipped — this pass gave it a real backend and a real grid, and it's deliberately not visible on anyone's profile but your own.** `saved_posts` (`user_id`, `post_id`, composite PK) is plain self-row RLS on `SELECT`/`DELETE`, matching `post_likes`'s own openness posture on everything except who can *see* a save — but unlike a like, a save is never shown to anyone, not even the post's own author, the same restraint Instagram itself applies. The `INSERT` policy adds one more check beyond self-row: `smim_videt_prispevek(post_id)` (the same `SECURITY DEFINER` helper the likes/comments RLS fix above introduced), so a client can't save a post it was never allowed to see in the first place — not a real attack surface on its own, but cheap insurance now that the helper already exists.

**Reading the saved list needed its own `SECURITY DEFINER` function for a second reason beyond `posts` having no plain `SELECT` policy: a save has to keep respecting *current* visibility, not just the visibility that held at save time.** `nacti_ulozene_prispevky()` joins `saved_posts` to `posts`, filtered to the caller's own rows, and re-checks `smim_videt_prispevek(p.id)` on every read — a post saved from an account that later blocks you, or goes private and drops you as a mutual follow, silently stops appearing in Uloženo even though the `saved_posts` row itself is untouched. This mirrors the exact non-retroactive-vs-ongoing distinction Secret Chat's blocking enforcement already drew ("blocking stops what happens next, it doesn't erase what was already sent") — here inverted: the *save* itself isn't erased, but its ongoing visibility is re-derived every time, never cached as a one-time grant.

**The save toggle lives in `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s like row, a bookmark icon (`SocialIcon` gained `bookmark`/`bookmark-filled`, same "filled = active" convention as `heart`/`heart-filled`) pushed to the row's right edge via `margin-left: auto`** — Instagram's own familiar placement, and no CSS change needed to the row itself since it was already a flex container. `jeUlozenyPrispevek()` is a plain self-row check (no function needed — asking "did *I* save this" is always your own row, unlike listing what a stranger saved, which this feature never builds), loaded alongside `vztah`/`komentare` in the same effect. `ulozitPrispevek()` is idempotent the same way `pridatLajk()`/`sledovatUcet()` already are — a repeat save hits the composite primary key's `23505` and is treated as success, not an error.

**`ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`'s "Uloženo" tab loads on first open, not eagerly with the rest of the page** — the same "on demand" restraint `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx`'s own `vztah`/`spolecni` already established, since most visits to your own profile never open this tab at all. Its grid reuses the exact `.social-prispevek-dlazdice` tile markup the foto/video tabs already use, minus the "add" tile (you save from browsing, never by uploading directly into Uloženo). Closing a post opened from this tab re-fetches the saved list — unsaving from inside the detail view (the bookmark toggle) has to be reflected back in the grid, and a stale tile lingering in Uloženo after its own unsave button was just pressed would read as broken.

**Wiring this up surfaced a real, pre-existing bug in `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx`: `PrispevekProhlizec`'s `jeMoje` prop was hardcoded `true`, which was harmless right up until Uloženo could show someone else's post for the first time.** Every post the "foto"/"video" tabs had ever shown genuinely was the account's own, so the hardcoded value was never wrong in practice — but a saved post is, by definition, usually somebody else's, and showing a delete button on a post you don't own (that would then fail server-side, or worse silently no-op) is exactly the kind of latent bug that only a new call site exposes. Fixed to `otevrenyPrispevek.autorId === stav.mujId`, computed per-post like every other caller of this component already does.

**The "Uloženo" tab existed as an identical stub a second time, on `VerejnyProfilDialog.tsx` — a stranger's own profile — and that copy is removed outright, not implemented.** It never made conceptual sense there: a save is inherently viewer-specific ("what *I* saved"), not a property of whichever profile happens to be open, and Instagram itself has no "Saved" tab on anyone's profile but your own. Leaving a working "Uloženo" tab on your own profile next to a permanently-stubbed one on everyone else's would have read as a bug report waiting to happen, not a design choice — the fix is one fewer tab on that dialog (`tab` narrowed from `'foto' | 'video' | 'ulozeno'` to `'foto' | 'video'`), not a second, meaningless implementation.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a genuine save of a real (public) post round-trips through `nacti_ulozene_prispevky()` correctly; the post's own owner cannot read another account's `saved_posts` row (self-row RLS); after the saver is blocked by the post's owner, the same saved post silently drops out of `nacti_ulozene_prispevky()`'s results without the underlying row being touched; saving a nonexistent/invisible post id is rejected by the `INSERT` policy's `smim_videt_prispevek` check; `anon` is rejected outright on `nacti_ulozene_prispevky()` and can't call it via RPC. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): saving a stranger's post from their profile flips the bookmark to filled; the same post then appears in the account's own "Uloženo" grid; opening it from there correctly offers "Nahlásit příspěvek," never a delete option, since it isn't the viewer's own post. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Story replies and reactions had never existed — stories only ever tracked views — and the fix is deliberately *not* a second storage system, just one more nullable column on `messages`.** `messages.story_id` (nullable, `on delete set null`, no extra RLS check — the exact same trust-the-client shape `reply_to_id` itself already has, since neither is security-relevant: `messages`' own `INSERT` policy already fully governs who may post into which chat regardless of what either reference column claims) is what a "reply" and a "reaction" both are, and the app makes no distinction between them at all — a reaction is just a reply whose body happens to be one emoji, sent by a one-tap shortcut instead of typing, mirroring how Instagram's own story reactions are themselves ordinary DMs, not a separate stored-reaction system the way `message_reactions`/`post_likes` are for chat messages and posts.

**Because a story is only ever visible to a mutual, non-blocked friend (`stories`' own `SELECT` policy), replying to one never has to think about the message-request path at all — `otevritChat()` reused as-is, no new founding logic needed.** A 1:1 chat with a non-friend lands as a pending request; a chat with someone whose story you can currently see is, by construction, always a mutual friend, so `zaloz_chat()` always takes the "already friends" branch and the chat (new or reused) lands `'prijato'` immediately. `reagovatNaStory(storyId, autorId, text)` is a thin two-step wrapper — `otevritChat()` then `poslatZpravu(chatId, text, null, null, storyId)` — not a new RPC, because nothing about sending a story-tagged message needed server-side trust beyond what already exists.

**The reply bubble shows a fixed generic label ("↩️ Reakce na story"), never a fetched thumbnail of the actual story, on purpose — not a missing feature, a deliberate restraint already established elsewhere in this file.** `reply_to_id`'s own quoted-message preview only ever searches the chat's *already-loaded* messages, never issues a second query per quote ("browse what's already loaded, don't multiply round-trips for a preview") — a story reply has nothing analogous to browse, since the story isn't part of the loaded message list at all, and by the time most replies are actually read, the 12–24 h story itself has very likely already expired and vanished. Fetching a thumbnail that will fail to resolve for most replies wasn't worth the extra per-bubble query; the generic label is honest about what's actually still there.

**Typing a reply pauses the story's own auto-advance timer, the same "don't interrupt someone mid-input" instinct as Pomodoro's warning-suppression and Secret Chat's typing indicator, but simpler: a derived boolean, not a new event.** `pozastaveno` is just `odpoved.trim().length > 0` — no separate "is focused" tracking needed, since a non-empty draft is itself proof the user is in the middle of something the auto-timer shouldn't interrupt. The `useEffect` that arms the per-slide `setTimeout` now returns early while `pozastaveno`, so no timer gets scheduled at all rather than one being scheduled and then raced against; tapping the left/right regions to navigate manually still works while typing, only the *automatic* advance is suppressed. A draft doesn't survive moving to a different slide in the same group (cleared in the same effect that already resets the signed URL and records a view per-slide) — replying to slide 1 with a half-typed sentence and then swiping to slide 3 should never silently send that sentence as a reply to slide 3.

**The quick-reaction row reuses `EMOJI_REAKCI` from `types.ts` verbatim — the same fixed seven-emoji set message reactions already use — rather than a second, story-specific constant.** Both are the identical "pevná sada, ne libovolný vstup" restraint this file already states for `IKONY_SKUPIN`/message reactions; inventing a slightly different set for stories would be a second list to keep in sync with the first for no real benefit. The reply input and emoji row are hidden entirely on your own story (`jeMoje`) — Instagram doesn't offer "reply to yourself" either, and that slot already shows the "N zhlédnutí" button instead.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing, network-request inspection rather than a full chat-list round trip — a leaner, more direct check of the actual new code path than seeding every table `nactiChaty()` reads): tapping a quick-reaction emoji on a mutual friend's story fires the expected `POST /rest/v1/messages` with `body` set to that emoji, `story_id` set to the story's id, and `chat_id` set to whatever `zaloz_chat` returned, and the UI shows "Odesláno." A second spec seeded a message with `story_id` directly and confirmed `ChatView.tsx` renders the "↩️ Reakce na story" label for it. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**A message request's media went straight to the screen the instant the recipient opened the chat, before they had decided whether to accept it at all — the same "explicit photo from a stranger" risk the message-requests feature itself never actually closed off, only made reachable.** Text stays visible in a pending request (deciding whether to accept, and this app's own "replying is itself acceptance" mechanism, both require reading it), but media is the part with no equivalent need — nobody has to *see* an unsolicited photo/video/voice message to decide whether to accept the conversation it arrived in. `ChatView.tsx`'s media-rendering branch now checks `chat.pozadavek && !moje`: an incoming request's media from the *other* side renders a plain locked placeholder ("Médium se zobrazí po přijetí žádosti") instead of `ZpravaMedium`/`ZpravaHlasovka`, while media the viewer sent themselves (as the request's founder, waiting on the other side) is never hidden from its own sender — there's nothing to protect them from. The gate is `chat.pozadavek` itself, the same field the pending-request gold banner already reads, so accepting the chat (via the banner's button, or simply replying, which auto-accepts through the pre-existing `prijmi_pozadavek_po_zprave` trigger) makes every already-sent media message reveal itself on the very next render — no separate "unlock" step, no re-fetch, since nothing about the message rows themselves ever changed, only which branch the same `chat.pozadavek` condition takes.

**This needed no backend change at all — it's a pure client-side display gate, the same shape as the composer already disabling attach/mic while editing a message.** The message and its `media_path` were always fully loaded (RLS already permits reading it once you're a chat member, `'pozadavek'` included — the whole point of that status is you can already see enough to decide), so this isn't access control; it's the same restraint already documented for Secret Chat's own notification body ("an OS notification tray isn't a secure surface, and putting plaintext there would undercut the design this feature was built around") — here, a chat screen isn't the place to render media from someone whose right to talk to you hasn't been decided yet, even though the bytes were always retrievable to a client that went looking for them directly.

**Verified via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): a mocked pending request with an incoming photo message shows the locked placeholder and no `<img>`/`<video>` element rendered at all; tapping "Přijmout" on the gold banner makes the placeholder disappear and the real image render in its place, on the same message row, with no page reload. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**This closes every item from this session's Social-improvement backlog** (report a post, like/comment notifications, double-tap-to-like, remove-follower plus a real followers/following list, saved posts, story replies/reactions, and hiding message-request media) — each verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation where it touched RLS, and via a temporary Playwright spec deleted after passing, with `npm test`/build kept clean and `auth.spec.ts`'s routing regression check unaffected throughout.

**`PratelePanel.tsx`'s friends list was pulled off the real profile page again, on request — `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` now stops right after "MŮJ KÓD," the same "display choice about what the newly-crowded profile page shows today, not a decision that the feature doesn't belong there" reasoning this file already recorded for "Moje úspěchy"/"Moje cíle" above.** `PratelePanel.tsx` itself is untouched and still does real work — it's still the friends list `SledujiciDialog.tsx` and the game hub's own patterns reuse, and it's still reachable inside Social itself (chats, search); only the import and the one render call in `ProfilSocialniSekce.tsx` are gone. `otevritSocial`/`setOtevrenyProfil` stayed, since `VerejnyProfilDialog`/`SkenovatKodDialog`/`SledujiciDialog` on this same page still need them. Verified with `npm run typecheck`/`test:unit`/`build` (clean) and a temporary Playwright spec (deleted after passing) confirming `/profil` still shows "MŮJ KÓD" but no "PŘÁTELÉ" section or friend row; `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Replies to comments were the top item on a second improvement pass, and building them surfaced a real, already-shipped correctness bug rather than just adding the new feature — a comment from a non-friend account on a public post was silently dropped, not merely un-attributed.** `nactiKomentare()` used to be a plain `.from('post_comments').select(...)` joined client-side to `nactiProfily()` (itself a plain `profiles` read) — but `post_comments`' own `INSERT` policy already lets *anyone signed in* comment on a visible public post via `smim_videt_prispevek()`, with no friendship requirement, while `profiles`' `SELECT` policy only grants visibility through `je_muj_pritel()`, a chat co-membership, or a **pending** follow — never an ordinary stranger. A stranger's comment on a public post therefore had its author profile resolve to nothing, and `.filter((k): k is Komentar => k !== null)` quietly dropped the whole comment — not a display glitch, a comment that existed in the database and simply never appeared to anyone viewing the post. Caught by building the exact same "does the recipient's own visibility of the *content* actually extend to visibility of who *wrote* it" check this session had already run into twice this pass (`nacti_aktivitu_prispevku`, `nacti_ulozene_prispevky`) and testing it directly rather than assuming the existing code was fine.

**The fix and the new feature share one `SECURITY DEFINER` function, `nacti_komentare_prispevku(post_id)`** — joining `post_comments` to `profiles` internally (the same elevation shape `nacti_aktivitu_prispevku`/`nacti_ulozene_prispevky` already established) so a comment's author name resolves regardless of friendship, gated only by `smim_videt_prispevek(post_id)` on the post itself. `post_comments.reply_to_id` (nullable, self-referencing, `on delete set null`) rides along in the same read, needing no extra RLS validation of its own — the identical "trust the client, the real access boundary is elsewhere" shape `messages.reply_to_id`/`messages.story_id` already established, since which comment a reply claims to answer isn't a privilege-relevant fact.

**Threading is deliberately one level deep, never recursive — the same flat-thread depth Instagram itself uses, and the reason `Komentar.replyToId` only ever needs one grouping pass, not a tree.** `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` splits the already-fully-loaded comment list into root comments (`replyToId === null`) and a `Map` of replies keyed by parent id, then renders each root followed immediately by its own replies, indented (`.social-prispevek-komentar--odpoved`, `margin-left` only, no separate component). Tapping "Odpovědět" on a reply itself attaches the new comment to that reply's *own* root (`k.replyToId ?? k.id`), not to the reply — so the thread can never grow a second level no matter how many times someone replies to a reply. A parent comment being deleted doesn't orphan its replies or cascade-delete them: `on delete set null` promotes them back to root comments automatically, the same graceful-degradation shape `ON DELETE SET NULL` already gives `messages.story_id` once its story expires and gets cleaned up.

**The reply-target banner reuses `.social-odpoved-lista` verbatim from `ChatView.tsx` rather than a second, comment-specific banner style** — "replying to X, tap ✕ to cancel" is the identical interaction in both places, and the composer's placeholder switches from "Napiš komentář…" to "Odpověz {jméno}…" the same way `ChatView.tsx`'s own reply flow already re-labels its input.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a genuinely non-friend account's comment on a public post now resolves its author's name through `nacti_komentare_prispevku()` (confirming the fix, not just the new column); a reply's `reply_to_id` round-trips correctly; `anon` is rejected outright on the function (not silently empty) and can't call it via RPC. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing): tapping "Odpovědět" shows the reply banner and re-labels the composer, and submitting posts the expected `reply_to_id` in the request body; a second spec confirmed an already-existing reply renders indented under its root comment. `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**Comment likes were the second item on the same improvement pass — a like existed on the post itself but not on any one comment underneath it, unlike Instagram where a thoughtful reply can get its own small heart.** `comment_likes` (`comment_id`, `user_id`, composite PK) is deliberately narrower than `message_reactions` — no `chat_id`-style denormalized column, because that column exists there purely so Realtime `postgres_changes` can filter on it and so the RLS check avoids a subquery; comment likes have neither concern (no live subscription on comments at all, and the one `exists (select 1 from post_comments c where c.id = comment_likes.comment_id)` subquery is cheap and, critically, safe: `post_comments` already carries a real, working `SELECT` policy — `smim_videt_prispevek(post_id)` — so the subquery re-applies that same visibility check for free, the same way a subquery against `reports` was already safe for the reported-post moderator policy above. This is the *other* half of the RLS-subquery-elevation lesson this file keeps relearning: a subquery against a table with **no** applicable RLS for the querying role needs a `SECURITY DEFINER` wrapper (`posts` itself, `smim_videt_prispevek`/`autor_prispevku` exist because of exactly that); a subquery against a table that **already has** a correct plain policy just inherits it, no wrapper needed. The same `exists` clause does a second job in the `INSERT` policy: `and not je_blokovan_se_mnou(c.user_id)` blocks liking a blocked comment author's words, the same posture `message_reactions` already established for reacting to a blocked sender's message.

**`nacti_komentare_prispevku()` grew two more output columns (`pocet_lajku`, `lajkuji_ja`) computed with one `left join lateral` per comment, rather than a second round trip per comment or a second RPC** — the function already runs once per post open (`PrispevekProhlizec.tsx`'s "on demand, not for the whole grid" loading), and folding the like aggregate into the same query keeps that "one query, everything the screen needs" shape rather than N+1 queries for N comments. Changing the function's output column set required `drop function` before `create` — Postgres's `create or replace` refuses to change a function's return columns, unlike the earlier, purely additive `alter table` changes this session made to `messages`/`post_comments` elsewhere.

**The like button sits as a small heart at the end of each comment row, a sibling to the existing delete button, not a replacement for anything** — `pocetLajku > 0` shows a compact "N×❤️" next to "Odpovědět" rather than a separate line, keeping a comment row to roughly the same height whether or not it has likes. `prepnoutLajkKomentare()` follows the exact "always re-fetch from the server after an action" idiom `prepnoutLajk()` (the post-level like) already uses — `PrispevekProhlizec.tsx` already holds the full comment list in memory, so a full `nactiKomentare()` re-fetch after a like/unlike is cheap and keeps one source of truth, not a second optimistic-update code path to keep in sync with the server.

**Verified against real production Supabase data via role/JWT impersonation**: a genuine non-friend, non-blocked account likes a real comment and the count/`lajkuji_ja` resolve correctly for both the liker and the comment's own author; a double-like hits the composite primary key's `23505` and leaves exactly one row (idempotent, not an error); a blocked pair is rejected outright on `INSERT`; `anon` is rejected on `nacti_komentare_prispevku()` (not silently empty) and can't call it via RPC. **And via a temporary Playwright spec** (deleted after passing, seeded fixtures rather than a live toggle-and-refetch round trip through the mock — the mock's RPC responses are static per call, not derived from the underlying table state, so this proves the two real states and the actual outgoing request rather than a mock-only round trip): an unliked comment shows "Lajkovat komentář" with no count and posts the expected `comment_id`/`user_id` to `comment_likes` on tap; a comment seeded with `pocet_lajku: 3, lajkuji_ja: true` renders "3×❤️" and "Odebrat lajk komentáře". `npm test`/build clean, `auth.spec.ts` unaffected (no routing touched).

**A post carousel (several photos/videos in one post) was the biggest item on the second improvement pass, and it's deliberately built additive: `posts.media_path`/`media_type` keeps meaning exactly what it always did (the first item), so every existing consumer of that column needed zero changes.** `post_media` (`post_id`, `media_path`, `media_type`, `position`) holds only the *second and later* items — no migration of existing rows, and a plain single-photo post now and forever has zero `post_media` rows, the same "additive, not a rewrite" posture `messages.story_id`/`post_comments.reply_to_id` already established this session.

**`post_media` needed no plain `SELECT` policy at all — it's read exclusively from inside the three `SECURITY DEFINER` functions that already resolve a post's media (`nacti_prispevky`, `nacti_feed`, `nacti_ulozene_prispevky`), each of which grew one more output column, `dalsi_media` (a `jsonb` array via one `left join lateral` per post, ordered by `position`).** Changing an existing function's output column set needs `drop function` before `create` — `CREATE OR REPLACE` refuses when the return columns change — unlike the purely additive `ALTER TABLE` changes this session made elsewhere.

**The `INSERT` policy on `post_media` repeated, and this time caught immediately by testing it rather than assuming a subquery would work, the exact RLS-subquery-elevation mistake this file has now hit twice before on `posts` specifically (`post_likes`/`post_comments`'s original migration).** The first version wrote `with check (exists (select 1 from public.posts p where p.id = post_media.post_id and p.user_id = auth.uid()))` — and since `posts` carries no plain `SELECT` policy at all (every ordinary read goes through a `SECURITY DEFINER` function on purpose), that `exists` subquery silently found nothing for *anyone*, including a post's own owner trying to add a second photo to their own carousel. Caught on the very first impersonation test (the post's own owner's insert failed with `42501`), not discovered later — fixed by swapping the raw subquery for `autor_prispevku(post_id) = auth.uid()`, the same `SECURITY DEFINER` helper `post_likes`/`post_comments` already needed for the identical reason. The general lesson this file keeps re-deriving: a subquery against `posts` from another table's RLS always needs that helper: `posts` genuinely has no plain-RLS path for any role to peek through.

**On the client, uploading is one function for one photo or ten — `nahratPrispevek(soubory: File[], caption?)` replaces the old single-`File` signature, with a small `nahratMediumPrispevku()` helper factored out so the resize/upload step isn't duplicated between the first item (which goes to `posts`) and the rest (which go to `post_media` with `position` starting at 1).** `MAX_KARUSEL_POLOZEK = 10` mirrors Instagram's own cap, enforced client-side before any upload starts — not a database constraint, since nothing about exceeding it is a security concern, just a UX guardrail. The file `<input>` gained `multiple`; `PridatPrispevekDialog.tsx`'s single large preview only shows for one file, and switches to a horizontally scroll-snapping thumbnail strip (`.social-prispevek-karusel-nahledy`, the same scroll-snap idiom the game hub's own character carousel already established) the moment more than one file is picked — no new gesture code, native browser scroll does the swiping.
